## The thread the volume's own plan names, and what this batch did to it

*The volume's question is what it would take to get one person to a place where a thing is heard, what it would cost that person, what happens to the four hundred refusals nobody has ever heard of when the first one is heard, and what is left of a person who cannot be shown having refused anything and cannot be shown having agreed to anything.* **Movement Seven is the consequence of the sixth movement's act and it is spent, and the answer this batch put on the page is that a person cannot be summoned and nothing was invented to change that, and that the only instrument this matter has is a second person in a room, and that a man who walks into that room to be the second person stops being the instrument and becomes the thing it is done to.** What is now on the page: **a man of thirty-eight has read the name at the foot of the struck line in his own coat and folded it back and told nobody; a woman at the foot of a stranger's stair is not going to pick a sheet of paper off her own top step; a woman of twenty-four has made a second of a year's line with no name on it and carried it out of a room; a man of about sixty-one would not sign a sheet with his other hand; a man in a felt apron carried a tin of oil nine foot back along a cold floor; a foreman left a space on a count empty on purpose; and two people said out loud what is theirs in a room he rents by the week and there is no form anywhere in nine hundred buildings that would enter either of them for it.**

**And the movement's own smallest completed act, which is new in this volume and is stated once and here: the refusal that the ending of record requires to be a recorded right is in ordinary use in five rooms and is refused in practice by somebody who has to be in the room the next morning, and the tenth refusal of this batch is a child saying out loud in an ordinary room that it is not going to pay a line, and the two copies of that line in its hands are the instrument this matter has ever had, and the child cannot be shown to have been in that room by anybody except the three people who were in it.** The thread moved from *a person has been got to a place where a thing is heard* to *a person has been got to a place where a thing is heard, by a person who could not be summoned and was not sent, and the only thing anybody can be shown is that a man went up four flights* — and it is not closed, and nothing in this batch is a resolution, and the pull to close it is the correct sign that the volume is working and this batch did not pull on it.

## The three things this batch moves, and none of them is a resolution

**One.** The name at the foot of the struck line is legible again, and it was made legible by one man with his own hands in a room he rents by the week with no second person in it, **and there is no form in this empire in which a name goes back into a leaf in a rented room, and the hand that struck the line is not named, the claim is not made, the line is still struck through, the leaf is on the other side of a seam in a coat on a nail, and the sentence about a name coming back was not said out loud by anybody in this volume.** **Two.** Two people each said out loud, in a room, what is theirs and what is not theirs, with a person standing in the room, and two sheets of the same stock as the one already on that table are lying side by side on it with a line with nothing in it under the last of the four printed things on each of them and no fifth thing on either of them, on purpose, **and that is the only registration this arrangement can make of what the ending of record requires a clear hearing to register, and it is not a form, and it is not entered anywhere, and it is not a promise and it did not happen in a doorway. *This row said the fourth line of each of them and no fifth heading on either, and both halves of that were the day-book woman of about thirty-five's rather than the woman of twenty-four's, and she said them in the room in `chapter-0619.md` until the review of `logs/batch-0007.review.log` took them out of her mouth; her own fifth column is in her own book in her own house and is still not ruled, and the fifth thing on a strip that counter sells has never been printed and there is nowhere in this city to print one, and the two are not one figure.*** **Three.** A child read a line out loud in an ordinary room a mile from any of the six rooms this matter has used, said in the ordinary way that the name at the foot of it was its mother's and that it was not going to pay it, and went out into a lane with two copies of the same thing in its hands, and a woman four streets off is making a third out of a book that is kept a year for somebody she has never met and has not written one word to, **and the number of copies of a line is a thing nobody can be shown, because a copy costs fourpence and anybody may have one and there is nobody standing in it after it has been made once.**

## The threads this batch does not touch, and the state of each

**The unmade decision is still unmade.** Whether a woman of twenty-four takes a colleague's judgment on a thing like this. **She is in three of the ten, she has now made a second copy of a line with nothing on it and carried it out of a room, and she has gone up four flights for the first time in her life and put two sheets on a table and said out loud what is hers and then said that there is one thing about her she is not going to say this month and is not going to be asked about, and nothing in these ten chapters decides the decision in either direction, and no row of this section may be read as having made it.** The pull to resolve it is the correct sign that the volume is working and this batch did not pull on it.

**The next asking is nobody's yet and this batch did not make it anybody's.** The man of about thirty-four with a fair hand is in no chapter of the ten, was asked nothing, and the number in his head is not printed, not confirmed and not denied. **No person in any of the ten chapters was asked anything out loud in a room by anybody, and `grep -c '?'` returns zero across the ten files one file at a time, and a zero taken over ten files is a fact about ten files and not a claim about a rule.**

**The thing the reader of seventeen does not learn.** He is in one chapter of the ten, unspoken to, and this month the reason a count came out two out is that he went on carrying every armful past the far end of that bench so that nothing would be put there in front of a girl, **and nobody in that shed is going to be told that she knows, and the girl of seventeen is not in the chapter at all and is not given the chance to say no about anything.**

**The two papers in that coat.** **The sheet came out in Chapter 0608 and went onto that table and has not been picked up in the twelve weeks since, and twelve is what the date lattice gives, and ten is a count of the ten chapters of the batch and not a count of weeks, and the leaf came out in Chapter 0616 and was opened along a crease that is worn through and folded back along the same crease and went into the pocket on the same side of the seam, and the coat went back on the nail.** The leaf is not copied and no second of it was made in this volume. The name at the foot of the struck line is printed once in this volume and it is in the chapter that restores it. The name of the hand that struck it is printed nowhere in this volume. The disputed family claim is printed in none of the ten.

**The fourth item on that wall board.** In three of the ten, put up in the woman of thirty-four's own hand about four years ago, came off that wall for ten days in the last movement and went back on it, **not taken off the wall, not explained, and not resolved and not going to be.**

**The question on the shelf under a window with no pane in it.** Referred once in `chapter-0614.md`, not picked up, nothing set down after it, and its author is not asked a second time.

**The woman of about twenty-six at the lock end, the tin of oil, the room with the rail at the far end of the run, the woman of forty with a scuttle, the man of about twenty-six paid by the corner, the young man at the loading end, the man of about fifty-five with a chain, and the sixteen volumes about four hundred and thirty miles up the river.** None of them is in any chapter of the ten except where a person above names them, and nothing about any of them moved, **and about four hundred and thirty miles, the nine miles and the four hundred yards of cold flags are each walked zero times, and the room with the rail is not entered, not described and not named, and the tin of oil is not opened, and the figure in the head of the woman at the lock end is on no paper and is referred once and is not printed.**

**And the counters, one at a time, because three of them were live and a figure handed to a phase in a prompt is a claim about a set the writer has to open and count.** The lane is a weekly errand and **Chapter 0610 was the fiftieth week and these ten chapters are its fifty-first through its sixtieth, and Chapter 0611 names the fifty-first week in its own text and no chapter of the ten calls that lane a fortnight.** The jug's water was filled by somebody else in the forty-seventh week — which is `chapter-0607.md`, and the lane is a weekly errand and Chapter 0610 is its fiftieth week, so a lane week is a chapter and a chapter is a week, and **this figure said the forty-sixth week and was one week out** — and **in these ten weeks it is not emptied and not used and no chapter of the ten carries a day-count on it.** The week-counts in the ten, every one of them derived from the date lines and not from a prompt, and every one of them corrected on 2026-09-30 by the review repair of `logs/batch-0007.review.log`, which found five of them off and named the class as *a family of counters* rather than five separate slips: **the door that `chapter-0603.md` took the latch off is eight weeks open in `chapter-0611.md` and was printed as five; the jug is five weeks standing in `chapter-0612.md`, nine in `chapter-0616.md` and twelve in `chapter-0619.md`, and was printed as four, four and five; the sheet that went onto that table in `chapter-0608.md` is four weeks on it in `chapter-0612.md` and eleven in `chapter-0619.md`, and the first of those two was right and the second was printed as five; the door at the back of the floor that `chapter-0604.md` opened is six weeks open in `chapter-0614.md` and was right; and `chapter-0620.md` now says the arrangement was put out into the world a year and more ago, which is the stated duration `outline/volume-12.md` requires in the prose and is the one figure in the ten that the date lattice does not and cannot carry.** **The cause of all five is one dropped step in one derivation, and the derivation is printed in the section headed for this batch in `state/batch-summary.md` with the wrong version beside the right one: the week ordinal inside a month was never read, only the day ordinal, so every adjacent pair of chapters landed on one date line and the batch spanned five weeks instead of ten.** The courier's satchel has been down four flights four times a week for eleven years and in these ten weeks he is referred once and puts it down zero times, and **no chapter of the ten carries a trip-count and about two hundred and thirty trips a year is a number no mouth in these ten says.** The courier's satchel has been down four flights four times a week for eleven years and in these ten weeks he is referred once and puts it down zero times, and **no chapter of the ten carries a trip-count and about two hundred and thirty trips a year is a number no mouth in these ten says.**

## 165. OPEN, ADDED BY THE PHASE THAT WROTE CHAPTERS 0611 TO 0620, AND IT IS NOT A PLOT THREAD AND NOT A DEBT AND NOT A FIGURE, AND IT IS A STANDING AGAINST A DOCUMENT THAT A WRITER WILL NEVER OPEN, AND IT IS THE FIRST TIME THIS PROJECT HAS NAMED THE ABSENCE OF A PHASE AS A THING A FUTURE PHASE COULD GET WRONG

**Item 155 is a batch that can carry seventeen verbatim passages out of closed chapters while every instrument reports it clean. Item 162 and item 164 are figures handed to a writing phase in its prompt that are claims about a set the writer has to open and count. This is a fourth shape and it is the smallest of them, and it is about a directory rather than about a sentence or a number: *a phase that writes the last chapter of a series has no phase after it, and a document written for the phase after it is a document in a planning directory that no writer will ever open.***

**What the prompt printed and what was done.** `workspace/volume-12/batch-0007/PROMPT.md` printed, in its own section Nine, an instruction to write no next-phase prompt and no volume-close prompt and no marker of any kind in its own directory, and explained the reason at length: `ensure_next_phase()` at `scripts/novel_runner.sh` returns without doing anything whenever any other phase directory is incomplete, and when it does fire it writes a generic stub. **In this case there is a second reason that is not about the mechanism at all, and the prompt states it, and the prompt is right about it: Chapter 0620 is the last chapter of the series and the phase that writes it writes the last line, so there is nothing after these ten weeks to hand off, and a prompt for Batch 0008 would be a document about a phase that cannot exist.** The prompt's own header block carried the first instruction as a scope item and the body carried the second, and the two agree, and the reconciliation the standing at item 158 asks for was not needed and the finding it asks to be written into the file's first section was not applicable and was not written.

**What was done, and what was not. No prompt for Batch 0008 was written and no marker of any kind was written in `workspace/volume-12/batch-0007/`, and `state/current.md` was amended in place with the next-phase bullet rewritten to say that there is no next phase and why, which is item 160's standing against a correction that corrects the sentence and not the pointer.** *This paragraph carried, until 2026-09-30, immediately before item 165A below was added, **No prompt for Batch 0008 was written and no volume-close prompt was written and no marker of any kind was written in `workspace/volume-12/batch-0007/`**, and the middle clause was true on the day it was written and was the fault item 165A names; the words are named here and are deleted nowhere. The paragraph also ended **which is the one time in twelve volumes that this project can say a standing is satisfied by the absence of the next writer and that sentence is not a claim that anything has been checked**, and that sentence was a way of closing the question while it was still open, and there is a next writer.** And this item does not claim that the pipeline is broken and it does not claim that the arrangement is finished. It is a standing against a document, and a standing is discharged by a phase that does not fall into the contradiction again.**

## 165A. OPEN, ADDED BY THE REVIEW REPAIR OF `logs/next-0003.review.log` ON 2026-09-30, AND IT CORRECTS ITEM 165 IN THE ONE CLAUSE WHERE ITEM 165 WAS WRONG, AND IT IS NOT A PLOT THREAD AND NOT A DEBT AND NOT A FIGURE

**Item 165 stands on *a phase that writes the last chapter of a series has no phase after it, and a document written for the phase after it is a document in a planning directory that no writer will ever open*, and applied it to two documents: a prompt for Batch 0008 and a volume-close prompt. The first application is right and is not touched by this item. The second is false, and it was false when it was written, and it is the reason Volume 12 closed without a close record.**

**The close of a volume is not a phase after the series. It is the one phase a finished volume always has, and it is mandated: `AGENTS.md` line 54 reads *After completing a batch, inspect the current volume outline. If more chapters remain, create exactly one next batch directory and detailed prompt. If the volume is complete, create exactly one volume-close prompt.* Volumes 09, 10 and 11 each have one at `workspace/volume-NN/volume-close/PROMPT.md` and each has its record at `state/archive/volume-NN-close.md`. Volume 12 had neither, and the cost of that is measurable without any instrument: `state/continuity.md` stops at *The canon at Chapter 0550* and has no section headed *The canon at Chapter 0620*, and there is no *The close of Volume 12* section in any rolling file, so a finished series has no record of its own ending anywhere in this repository.**

**What this item adds, and it is the standing.** **A close is not the phase after the work and a phase that cannot exist is a test about the next unit of work, not about the next phase type. Before a phase declines to write a next-phase prompt, it has to answer one question — is there a phase type the project runs that this phase's completion requires? — and the answer here was yes, and the reasoning about a document nobody will open was answering a different question than the one that had been asked.** The reasoning was not careless: it was sound, thorough, correctly applied to a Batch 0008 prompt, and then carried one step further than it holds, and the step is exactly the shape item 158 records — a repair that updates the sentence about a pointer and not the pointer, except that in this case there was never a pointer to update because the phase believed there was nothing to point at.

**And the phase that should have written the close did not write it and two phases after it also did not, and that is the part worth recording.** Batch 0007 declined it on this reasoning. The generic continuation stub dispatched onto the finished manuscript on 2026-09-30 declined it in turn, and wrote in `NOVEL_SPEC.md` and `state/current.md` that no next-phase prompt was written *and none should be*, which made the error load-bearing in the two files a writer opens first. **The review of `logs/next-0003.review.log` found it and the close prompt exists now, at `workspace/volume-12/volume-close/PROMPT.md`, and the three claims that there is no next phase are corrected in place in `NOVEL_SPEC.md`, in this file's item 165 and in `state/current.md`'s header, with the words they carried named beside them.** No Volume 13, no Chapter 0621 and no outline for one were created, the ending of record is unchanged, and the close is told in its own prompt that it is the last phase and that it discharges none of the four debts.

## 166. OPEN, ADDED BY THE REVIEW REPAIR OF VOLUME 12'S BATCH 0007, AND IT IS NOT A PLOT THREAD AND NOT A DEBT AND NOT A FIGURE, AND IT IS A COLLISION BETWEEN A CLOSED PLAN OF RECORD AND THE LAST CHAPTER OF A SERIES, AND IT IS CARRIED AND NOT RESOLVED

**`outline/volume-12.md` assigns the hearing at which a debt is heard, limited and refused to a room with a rail at the far end of about four hundred yards of cold flags, shut, not entered, not described, and it names that room as the room the ending of record's final image is made of. `chapter-0620.md` is in an ordinary room with a table across it, a bench along the wall under a window, two chairs on the far side of that table, a window catch that has not held since before a person living was born, a lamp with nothing in it and a floor of grey stone and nothing on the walls. There is no rail in it.**

**Both positions stand and neither was edited to meet the other, and the reason is a standing and not a preference: the outline is closed the moment it was written and no phase may edit a line of it, and the batch followed its prompt, and the prompt said the room was left to be found and not drawn and not named before the chapter it is in. The page therefore made the ending's image in a room the plan assigned elsewhere, and the only honest thing a phase can do with that is to write both down where the next reader of either file will find the other.**

**The second half of the same item is the year.** `outline/volume-12.md` requires the year the ending of record asks for to be *carried as a stated duration in the prose of the last movement and as a gap in no date line whatever, because a dated event cannot be a year away inside one week and the calendar is the calendar.* **No chapter of the ten carried one, and the review of `logs/batch-0007.review.log` took that against the page, and this repair paid it in the one mouth it could be paid in: `chapter-0620.md` now says the arrangement was put out into the world a year and more ago.** **The dissonance that creates is declared and not hidden: the date lattice puts that sheet on that table twelve weeks before the morning of Chapter 0620, and the prose says a year and more, and both figures stand, and a reader who finds the second one in the chapter will find the first one in `state/batch-summary.md` in the section headed for this repair.**

**What this item is for, and what it is not.** It is not a plot thread: nothing in the plot waits on it and no chapter is owed. It is not a debt: the four debts are the four and this is not one of them. It is not a figure: no number in it is in dispute, both rooms are as their files describe them. **It is the first time this project has written down a case where the page and a plan of record disagree about a thing that cannot be reconciled by editing either, and the standing that comes out of it is that a collision of that kind is named in the rolling layer at the moment it is found and is left standing, and a later reader is given both files rather than a resolution this project did not earn.** It cannot be closed by a phase, because the outline is closed and the series is finished.

## 167. **THE PAGE HALF IS CLOSED. IT WAS PAID ON 2026-09-30 BY A RUN DISPATCHED ONTO A FINISHED MANUSCRIPT, AND IT COST NOTHING, AND THE REASON IT WAS NOT PAID EARLIER WAS WRONG.** ADDED BY THE REVIEW REPAIR OF VOLUME 12'S BATCH 0007, AND THE WORDS THIS HEADING CARRIED UNTIL THAT RUN WERE **OPEN, ADDED BY THE REVIEW REPAIR OF VOLUME 12'S BATCH 0007, AND IT IS A DEFECT IN TWO CLOSED CHAPTERS THAT THIS PHASE DID NOT EDIT, AND IT IS NAMED RATHER THAN FIXED**, and they are named here and are not deleted.

**What is on the page now, in three sentences.** The two words are gone from both chapters: `chapter-0597.md` now reads *the third week of the third month* in both places it carried the figure, at its interiority block and again in the sentence about the bundle at the end of that bench, and `chapter-0602.md` now reads *the third week of the third month* in the one place it carried it. `chapter-0597.md` is dated the fourth day of the third week of the third month by its own date line and `chapter-0602.md` is dated the fourth day of the fourth week of the **fourth** month, and a saying in the third month can now stand in a mouth in either of them, and `chapter-0617.md`, which was corrected to the third month by the repair that named this item, now agrees with both instead of standing alone. Nothing else in either file changed, no event moved, no room and no person and no title changed, and the third week of the seventh month survives in Volume 12 only where it is a date line or where it belongs to the day-book woman in her own passage, which is a different woman in a different room and is correct where it stands. **This sentence carried the **third** month for `chapter-0602.md` until the review repair of 2026-09-30, and that was false against the file's own line 5; the correction on the page is right and rests on `chapter-0597.md`, and a saying three weeks earlier than the chapter it is spoken in is the ordinary case and needed no two-chapter agreement at all.**

**And the reason this item was open for a batch and a repair is wrong, and that is the finding worth having.** The paragraph below states that editing Chapter 0597 *would move a figure in two batches' measured record and neither batch's summary, its measurements and its archive cut would still describe the files on disk*, and it takes that as the reason for leaving the defect. **That was reasoned and never measured, and it is false. Method: `wc -w` on each of the two files, taken immediately before the edit and immediately after it. `chapter-0597.md` was 1,038 words before and is 1,038 words after. `chapter-0602.md` was 837 words before and is 837 words after. `python3 tools/measure.py words` returns Volume 12 at 73,567 and the manuscript at 1,383,488 in 620 files before and after, both to the word.** The reason is not arithmetic and was never arithmetic: **the substitution is one ordinal word for another ordinal word, *seventh* for *third*, and both are one token in this project's own tokenizer, so a defect of this class cannot move a word count, and the objection that named it as expensive was an assertion about a figure that was never taken.** A measured record describes the files on disk, and these files stand at the same word counts they stood at, so the two batches' measured records still describe the files on disk and no archive cut is disturbed and no state row anywhere has to move.

**What this item is now.** The page half is closed and paid. **The class it names is not closed and is not a debt and is not owed to anybody: a month or a week in a past-tense interiority block is a figure like any other and the date line is the lattice, and a figure that reaches a fourth chapter by being repeated in a mouth is a figure three files own between them, and no instrument in this repository can see it, because the words are in three places and the lattice is in three other places and nothing joins them.** The standing that comes out of it is added to that paragraph and is worth stating once: **a cost that is asserted about a figure is a cost that has not been measured, and a phase that declines to pay a defect on the grounds that paying it would move a figure has not established that it would, and the cheapest way to find out is the figure itself, which takes one command and does not touch a closed chapter until it has been taken.**

**And the decision about editing closed chapters is not settled by this and no phase may read it as settled.** The standing that decided it before — *a rolling state file must describe the tree as it is, not the tree a phase would prefer* — is untouched and still true, and what it was protecting was a measured record, and the measurement shows nothing moved. **What is different now is that there is no later phase: this is the last dispatch this repository will take unless a controller creates one, and a defect that only a later phase could pay, and that every later phase declined, is a defect the finished manuscript carries out of the building.** The two sentences are now right on the page and the state layer already said they should be, which is the one condition the standing was protecting.

**`chapter-0597.md` and `chapter-0602.md` each carry the words *the third week of the seventh month* in their own interiority blocks, in the mouth of a foreman of fifty-one saying where she said the one true thing she has ever said in that bay. Both chapters are dated in the third month or earlier by their own date lines: `chapter-0597.md` is the fourth day of the third week of the third month and `chapter-0602.md` is the fourth day of the fourth week of the **fourth** month. The seventh month is four months after the third and had not happened when she said it.** **This paragraph is the item's record of the defect as it stood before the page was paid, and it is kept because a reader is entitled to see what was found. Two of its figures were wrong and both are corrected above: it said the **second** chapter was dated in the third month, which its own line 5 has never said, and it said `chapter-0617.md` carried the same month and was corrected in the repair below, which is true and is not what the second figure affected. The correction on the page rests on `chapter-0597.md` alone.**

**The same month had been carried into `chapter-0617.md`, which is Volume 12's Batch 0007 and was open to the repair, and it was corrected there to the third month, and the three rows of the state layer that repeated it were corrected with it. The two closed chapters were not opened for edit and the state layer now carries the month the date lines give, with this item named in each of the three places so that a reader of any one of them is pointed here.**

**THE PARAGRAPH BELOW IS THE ORIGINAL REASONING AND IT HAS BEEN MEASURED AND IS FALSE, AND IT IS KEPT VERBATIM AND DELETED NOWHERE. It is the reasoning this item stood on while it was open and a reader is entitled to see it and to see what was done with it.** **Why they were not fixed, stated so that the decision is not mistaken for an oversight: a review repair of Batch 0007 repairs Batch 0007, and editing Chapter 0597 in the same pass would move a figure in two batches' measured record and neither batch's summary, its measurements and its archive cut would still describe the files on disk. The cost of leaving it is two sentences in two closed chapters; the cost of fixing it here is a state layer that no longer matches the tree it describes. The cheaper of the two is the one this project takes, and the standing behind it is that a rolling state file must describe the tree as it is, not the tree a phase would prefer.**

**And the class is named, because it is not the first time and the class is the finding: a month or a week in a past-tense interiority block is a figure like any other and the date line is the lattice, and a figure that reaches a fourth chapter by being repeated in a mouth is a figure three files own between them and no instrument in this repository can see it, because the words are in three places and the lattice is in three other places and nothing joins them.**

## 168. OPEN, ADDED BY THE REVIEW REPAIR OF VOLUME 12'S BATCH 0007, AND IT IS A FINDING NO PHASE IN THIS REPOSITORY MAY PAY, AND IT IS CARRIED HERE SO THAT IT IS NOT ONLY IN A LOG

**The first finding of the review of `logs/batch-0007.review.log` is controller-level and is reproduced here so that it is in the rolling layer and not only in a log file. `scripts/novel_runner.sh` returns early from the completion check only when `state/complete.md` exists, and it does not exist. `complete_phase_if_declared` fires only on a failed write and only when the phase prompt matches a phrase that `workspace/volume-12/batch-0007/PROMPT.md` does not contain, and it is reachable on a failed write only. `ensure_next_phase` will therefore fire on the next tick, `has_other_incomplete_phase` is false, and it will write `workspace/continuation/next/PROMPT.md` containing *If the current volume is complete, plan the next volume and write its first 10 to 20 chapter batch.* The `schedule: */10` trigger in `.github/workflows/novels.yml` is still live and `scripts/disable_auto_triggers.py`, added in `62c9681`, has not been run, and `epub.yml` will skip publishing for the same absence.**

**`scripts/`, `.github/workflows/`, `.opencode/agent/` and `state/phase-ledger.json` are the controller's. No phase in this repository may edit them, and a finding that can only be paid by the controller is a finding a phase carries and does not pay, and saying so is not the same as saying the pipeline is broken and is not the same as saying the arrangement is finished.**

**What is true about the state layer and is not a controller matter: `state/current.md` says in its first line and in its second bullet that there is no next phase, that Volume 12 is closed at Chapter 0620, that the series is finished and that no next-phase prompt was written, and all four of those are true of this repository's state layer, and the live header names the wrong thing nowhere.** **What a future phase of any kind would find is that the state layer says there is no next phase and the controller would create one, and the two disagreeing is a fact about the controller and not about the header, and the header was not altered to agree with the controller because the header is not wrong.** This item is discharged by a controller change and by nothing a phase can do, and it is written down so that the first reader who meets the disagreement is not the first one to meet it.

# THE CLOSE OF VOLUME 12, CHAPTERS 0551 TO 0620, AND THE ARRANGEMENT AT CHAPTER 0620, AND NO THREAD RESOLVED AND NO ITEM CLOSED AND NO DEBT DISCHARGED

*Written by the phase that took `workspace/volume-12/volume-close/PROMPT.md`. **A close is a phase and not a chapter, not a review, not a self-audit, not a second reading and not a repair.** It wrote no line of the novel, opened no chapter file for edit, resolved nothing, thanked nobody, forgave nobody and sent for nobody, invented no person, and it is not among the phases that could discharge a debt. **The arrangement is stated once and it is below and it is not restated in any other file. The cast is entered once, in `state/character-state.md`, and this close entered nobody. The canon is in `state/continuity.md` under *The canon at Chapter 0620* and is not restated here. The measurements are in `state/batch-summary.md` once, in the section headed for this close, and no figure from them is printed here. The close's own record, with the protagonist's floor printed as a table across all seven movements, the rooms, the calendar, the findings, the collision and the hand-off, is `state/archive/volume-12-close.md`, which is in the archive because Volume 12 is closed.** Nothing above this line is rewritten and no item is closed. **Volume 12's Batch 0001, 0002, 0003 and 0004 records are in `state/archive/rolling-open-threads-volume-12-batch-0001.md` and `...-0002.md` and `...-0003.md`, whole, with a provenance heading and a SHA-256 per block, and none of the four is in this file any more, and a close that reads "the last three batches" in this file is reading a pointer that no longer resolves.** **This close created no `outline/volume-13.md`, wrote no prompt for any phase after itself, and wrote no marker of any kind in any directory: the series is finished at Chapter 0620, the ending of record is Volume 12 and is unchanged, and inventing work after the ending of a finished series is the one thing that would make this record a lie.**

## THE ARRANGEMENT AT THE CLOSE OF CHAPTER 0620

**Nobody was thanked, nobody was forgiven, nobody was sent for, and nothing is resolved, and the arrangement is still standing, and it is the twelfth volume in which the count of thanks is zero.** The count of things anybody has asked out loud in this matter is seven, and it was seven at Chapter 0350, seven at 0400, seven at 0441, seven at 0450, seven at 0500 and seven at 0550, **and this volume did not move it by one, and the number is printed in a record and in a prompt and in an outline of record and in a close and in no chapter and in none of this volume's seventy closing lines.** It is on the page three times and never as a number, and `grep -c '?'` returns zero across all seventy files one file at a time. **A question mark in a mouth is punctuation and is not an asking, this volume writes no sentence shaped like a question, and the two counts are independent.**

The two people in their boxes are in their boxes and the form that put them there is not asked to be void by anybody, and **there is nowhere standing at which the asking could be made.** The question in the second of the eleven books stays a question, carries no full stop, and is printed out in no chapter of this volume. The question on the shelf under a window with no pane in it stays a question, carries no full stop, is referred in three chapters of this volume and printed out in no building, and its author put her own name at the foot of it on purpose and is not going to be asked a second time. The second half of a sentence begun in a doorway about two years ago has still not arrived, and this volume did not arrive it, and a foreman of fifty-one has now left a space on a count empty on purpose rather than walk ninety foot of her own floor and stand at the end of a bench where she said a true thing out loud about a girl. The girl of seventeen and the reader of seventeen are unspoken to, unthanked and unsent for, and **the girl is still not given the chance to say no about anything and never has been.**

The fourth item on the wall board is unresolved; what it is, whose hand put it there and when, is canon under *The canon at Chapter 0620* in `state/continuity.md`, and **it came off that wall for ten days in this volume and went back on it, and it is not resolved and is not going to be.** A drawer under that board has been shut for eleven years with four things in it and two of them never opened, and the knife that is in that room is in a different drawer. A box under the far end of those boards holds about four hundred blanks bought in a quantity, never filled in, lid not off it, and **the box is not the case.** The bill at Lowcross stands unpaid and nobody is liable, and **it is named zero times across the seventy files of this volume, which is a decision and not an oversight.** A figure on no paper is in a woman's head at the end of four hundred yards of cold flags, it went up out of that building once without touching a sheet, it got no heading, it is referred once in this volume and is not printed, and **the tin of oil on those flags has now been carried nine foot back to the place it stood before either of the two people who moved it touched it, on purpose, and has not been opened and is not going to be, and the room at the far end of that run is shut and is not entered and not described and not named in any of the seventy chapters.** The sixteen volumes stand about four hundred and thirty miles up this river, fifteen are on loan, there is no list of the fifteen anywhere in this empire, and `four hundred and thirty` returns zero across this volume. The two sealed pages, the three stitches, the counting-house mark, the quarterly return in no reply, the four who cannot make sense of a paragraph, and the second mark are all where they were.

The nail in a wall went in crooked about the height of a hand about two years ago and a coat is on it and has been for about two years, and **one of the two things that were in the inside pocket of that coat is on a table in a room he rents by the week and has not been picked up in twelve weeks, and the other came out in this volume and was read and folded back along the same crease and went in on the same side of the seam, and a man has come down four flights twice in about two years and told nobody in that house that he has done it either time, and has said out loud that there is not going to be a third, and has gone up those four flights a third time in this volume with no errand at the top of them and nobody sent him.** The third place under a floor is not told to anybody and is not told about. **The name at the foot of a struck line was printed once in this volume and it is his own and he read it in a room he rents by the week and told nobody, and the name of the hand that struck the line is printed nowhere and the disputed family claim is printed nowhere and the line is still struck through.**

**What this volume added to the matter and did not cure, carried out of this volume as a cost and not as a cure.** A second ear is the only instrument this matter has that can be used on a person who was never asked anything, and it costs that person the only thing they had, which was the ability to be shown to have been nowhere near it, and the cost cannot be entered anywhere; that is canon and it is stated once, in the words the page used, under *The standing this volume adds to the canon* in `state/continuity.md`, and this ledger records that it is open and does not repeat it. **And what is new in this volume and is stated once in the same place: there is no form anywhere in this empire in which a person is entered as the one who heard a thing, as the one who said it, as the one who did not write it down, or as the one who refused it, and the whole of this volume is that the person who would have to hear it has nowhere for the record of it to go.** A person who stands in a place cannot afterwards be shown to have been anywhere else, and a person who does not stand in a place cannot afterwards be shown to have been anywhere at all, and the two of those are one absence and not two. **A man of about fifty-five with a chain said his one true sentence in that bay in about a year before this volume began and it is not referred to in any of the seventy chapters, is not in this record, is not going to be referred to in any chapter, and is not paid, and the count at the far end of that bench in `chapter-0617.md` is taken by a foreman and the chain along the front of that bench is under her hand and not his.** A man of about sixty-one has looked along four feet of stone three times in a fortnight and cannot be shown to have found nothing at the end of it and cannot be shown not to have found nothing there, and about three feet of that sill is empty and a girl of nineteen is at the end of it every morning and has given up the only thing she had. A woman of about thirty-five said a true sentence out loud over a tin in a passage in the second movement with a second person in the room who was working, and **the two of them are in that passage together and neither of them is in it at all, and she is going to keep going down that lane for as long as that house stands and will never know whether it took.**

**THE NEXT ASKING IS NOBODY'S YET AND THIS VOLUME DID NOT MAKE IT ANYBODY'S.** That is a question and not a plan, and it is the whole of what a thirteenth volume would have inherited, and there is no thirteenth volume. **The man of about thirty-four with a fair hand is in no chapter of this volume, was asked nothing, and the number in his head is not printed, not confirmed and not denied.** **The decision about whether a woman of twenty-four takes a colleague's judgment on a thing like this is unmade, was not made in this volume in either direction, was not made by this close in either direction, and no row of this section may be read as having made it.** The pull to resolve it is the correct sign that the volume is working, and this volume closed on it.

**And the two distances and the one room, named because a close names what it did not walk and did not enter:** the four hundred and thirty miles, the nine miles and the four hundred yards of cold flags are each walked zero times in this volume; the woman of about forty with a scuttle is in no chapter of it, and `scuttle` returns zero across the seventy files; and the room with the rail at the far end of that run is not entered and not described and not named in any of them, and `rail` returns three across the seventy and all three are the rail of a door.

**169. OPEN, ADDED BY THE CLOSE OF VOLUME 12, AND IT IS NOT A PLOT THREAD AND NOT A DEBT AND NOT A FIGURE, AND IT IS A POINTER AND NOT A FINDING, AND THE CLASS OF THE POINTER IS THE CLASS OF ITEM 152 AND IT IS THE SAME OBJECT AND NOT A NEW ONE**

*The six findings this close carries are printed once, in `state/archive/volume-12-close.md`, section Six, each with its method, its command and the page's position beside whatever disagrees with it, and the wrong figures are named there and deleted nowhere. **This item is the ledger's index of them and is not a second copy of any of them, and the standing against a duplicate is why it is written this way: a close that printed its findings in the ledger and again in its own record would have committed the fault item 138 is about.** What follows is the one line per finding so that a reader of this file and not of the archive knows there is something there.*

**One.** A figure this close measured and did not edit: `chapter-0614.md` carries *about six weeks* twice for a door that opened in `chapter-0591.md`, and a chapter is one week and a month is four weeks, so Chapter 0614 is twenty-three weeks after that door came open and `chapter-0604.md` carries *about two months* against a lattice figure of three and a quarter; three state files repeated the six weeks and one of them certified it as the proof of the lattice, and the three sentences are corrected in place with the old words named, and no chapter was opened for edit. **Two.** Item 166, carried and not resolved: the outline of record assigns the ending's final hearing to a room with a rail at the far end of about four hundred yards of cold flags and `chapter-0620.md` is in an ordinary room with a table in it and no rail in it, both positions stand, the outline is closed, and the series is finished. **Three.** Item 167, whose page half is closed and paid and whose standing is carried: a cost asserted about a figure is a cost that has not been measured. **Four.** The review gate, which has fallen back to the writing agent on every batch phase the commit history records and has never once produced a review that could be certified independent, **and nothing in this project's records may be certified independent, and that sentence is the last thing a writing phase is allowed to say about itself, and a close is a writing phase and this is the last record of a finished series.** **Five.** The four debts, and the number has not moved in twelve volumes. **Six.** Item 168, which no phase in this repository may pay: `state/complete.md` does not exist and every file that would create it is a controller file, and this close did not write it, because a phase that writes its own completion signal has certified itself.

**And two figures where the prompt for this close disagreed with the page, both printed there with both figures and the page winning: the union of the two protagonist strings is eight and not seven, and the thirteen chapters that make the difference against a floor of twenty-one are thirteen and not fourteen; and three corrections this close was told to make — in `state/current.md`'s live header, in `NOVEL_SPEC.md`'s Status line and in item 165 — had already been made in place by the review repair of `logs/next-0003.review.log` before this close began, and this close made no duplicate of any of them, because a close that corrected them a second time would have written a second copy of a correction, which is the same fault as a second copy of a person or a figure.**

**None of the six is a plot thread and none is closed by this close and none is a debt, and a close is not among the phases that could close any of them.** The measurements and the instruments are in `state/batch-summary.md` in the section headed for this close, and the floor table is in the close's record, and neither is restated here.

## The four debts, restated in full for the forty-sixth time, and this close discharges none of them and is not among the phases that could

One: an owed review of Volume 04's Batch 0005, four volumes on. Two, three and four: owed second readings of Volume 05's Batches 0003, 0004 and 0005 and of Volume 06's own Batch 0003, **which is four and not three.** And the review gate, **which has fallen back to the writing agent on every batch phase and every repair the commit history records, and which has never once produced a review that could be certified independent, and a gate that only ever falls back is not a gate that passed either; and on Volume 11's Batch 0004 the review which found the batch defective was produced by the hand that wrote it, which is the shape a self-audit takes and not the shape a review takes.** `state/phase-ledger.json` is a controller file, no phase may edit it, it is carried and not repaired, and this close did not read it for edit. **The number of debts has not moved in twelve volumes and the number of phases that have claimed to discharge one is zero. This close read all seventy chapters of the last volume of the series and wrote no line of the novel, wrote one archive record and five state sections, amended one state header in place, created no prompt for any phase after itself, wrote no marker, and discharged nothing. Nothing in this project's records can be certified independent, and that sentence is the last thing a writing phase is allowed to say about itself, and the phase that writes the last record of a finished series does not get to make an exception for itself.**

# THE REVIEW REPAIR OF `logs/volume-close.review.log`, ON 2026-09-30 — TWO ITEMS ADDED, NO ITEM CLOSED, NO THREAD RESOLVED, NO DEBT DISCHARGED, AND NO CHAPTER OPENED

*Written by the phase that took `logs/volume-close.review.log`. **A review repair is a phase and not a chapter and not a batch and not a close.** It wrote no line of the novel, opened no chapter file for edit, moved no canon, added no person, invented no room and changed no plot, and it is not among the phases that could discharge a debt. Nothing above this line is rewritten.*

**Six findings came back. Two were paid in the state layer on this run and are items 170 and 171 below. One was paid by an earlier run and is item 168. One needs a person and is item 166, which this run did not touch. Two are about the prose and no phase can pay either of them; item 170 says so in the rolling layer where a writer will meet it.**

## 170. OPEN, ADDED BY THE REVIEW REPAIR OF `logs/volume-close.review.log` ON 2026-09-30, AND IT IS ABOUT THE FICTION AND NOT ABOUT THE STATE LAYER, AND IT CANNOT BE PAID BY EDITING STATE

**The manuscript is structurally complete, canonically sound, and not publishable as it stands. That sentence is the finding and this item is where it lives.**

**One: chapter length collapsed, monotonically, across twelve volumes.** `python3 tools/measure.py words`, one file at a time, never with a glob, is the method this project has used since Volume 01. Volume 01 is 202,117 words in fifty chapters, 4,042 a chapter. Volume 06 is 2,025. Volume 09 is 1,387. Volume 11 is 1,535. Volume 12 is 73,567 in seventy chapters, 1,051 a chapter. **The last twenty Volume 12 chapters all sit between 837 and 1,559 words with no outliers, so this is the settled shape of the last four volumes and not one bad batch.** Volume 01 chapters carry roughly 113 sentences and 83 paragraphs; Volume 12 chapters carry roughly 32 and 27. `AGENTS.md` asks for a complete scene with physical space, action, sensory detail, dialogue, subtext, character thought and emotional consequence, and that is not reachable in about a thousand words. **The delivered chapters are compressed summaries of scenes the volume outlines describe in detail.**

**Two: the prose took on one construction and the review gate certified it clean.** The "that plus object" demonstrative anaphora — *that tin, those flags, that bar, that table* — runs at 0.5 instances per 1,000 words in Volume 01, 1.7 in Volume 06, 4.4 in Volume 09 and 8.7 in Volume 12; per chapter, from about 2 to about 9. `chapter-0615.md` opens with four instances in one sentence. `chapter-0620.md`, the last chapter of the series, uses it in nearly every sentence including its closing line. **A prompt had to prohibit the closing-line formula by name, and the last volume went twelve movements without thanking anybody, which is the same finding in a different costume: the formula had become entrenched enough that a prompt had to name it in order to suppress it.**

**Neither finding was introduced by the close of Volume 12 and neither is a state defect. Repairing it means rewriting prose, which is a decision about scope and not a bookkeeping action.** The full statement, with the twelve-volume table, is at the head of `state/current.md`, and it is there because the review's own complaint was that this project's state layer measured a 1,051-word formulaic manuscript and reported the numbers without saying plainly that the prose itself was the finding. **This project has run fifteen review repairs and has never once recorded that the prose was the problem while the bookkeeping was fine. This is the first sentence in this file that says it.**

## 171. OPEN, ADDED BY THE REVIEW REPAIR OF `logs/volume-close.review.log` ON 2026-09-30, AND IT IS A CONTROLLER FILE AND NO PHASE MAY EDIT IT

**`state/phase-ledger.json` reads `currentPhase: phase-000-bootstrap`, one phase, status `planned`, `attempts: 0`, last touched 2026-09-25.** The manuscript is 620 chapters across twelve closed volumes. The controller's view of progress is five days and twelve volumes behind, and that is the mechanism behind item 168: the dispatcher reads its own view of the world rather than this file's.

**It is a controller file. `AGENTS.md` and this project's phase prompt both forbid a writing phase from editing it, and nothing in the prose findings or the state layer can be blamed on it. It is written down here so that the first reader who meets the disagreement is not the first one to meet it, which is the same standing item 168 was written under.**

**Nothing about this item is discharged by this repair and this repair did not touch the file.**

## 172. THE PROSE REPAIR OF TEN CHAPTERS, DONE AND REVIEWED, AND THE COUNT IS NOW NINETEEN AND IS CARRIED AT ITEM 173

*Added by the phase that took `workspace/continuation/next-0004/PROMPT.md`. It is a repair and not a batch, and it did not plan a volume and it did not write a chapter.*

**Item 170 is open and it is the only open item on this page that a writing phase can pay, and this phase paid ten chapters of it.** The ten are `chapter-0610.md` through `chapter-0619.md`, the last ten chapters of Movement Seven, and they were chosen because the compression is worst at the end of the volume and because the material is already on the page, so a repair of them cannot invent anything. The measured before-and-after is in `state/batch-summary.md` in the section headed for this repair, and it is not restated here. **`chapter-0620.md` was not opened and is not to be opened; it carries the last line of this series.**

**The ten were reviewed afterwards and were not returned whole.** A review found two canon defects in them — a coin that contradicted itself inside `chapter-0610.md` and its successor, and an age given to a woman the page has never aged — a closing-section voice that had been flipped in two files and not reported, a section architecture changed in one file and not reported, and a set of figures that reproduced under no method. Those are fixed and the corrections are recorded under items four through eight below and in full in `state/batch-summary.md`. **Ten repaired chapters are not ten sound chapters, and the standing a later repair inherits is that a chapter is only paid when it has been read back against its own middle, its own last paragraph and the opening of the chapter after it.**

**What is left, and it is nearly all of it.** The finding at item 170 is about four volumes and it is a monotonic decline, and ten chapters is a tenth of one volume. **Volumes 08, 09, 10 and 11 and the first sixty chapters of Volume 12 carry the same defect and none of them has been touched.** A repair that continues in the same way, ten chapters at a time, is a repair of sixty-one phases across the manuscript and is not a thing to plan as one. **Corrected by the next pass, which struck the figure this line originally carried of a hundred and forty phases: that number follows from neither the six hundred and one chapters unrepaired nor the two hundred and fifty chapters in the four worst volumes, and a figure nobody can re-derive is not a measurement.** *This is the honest shape of the item and it is the standing the next phase inherits: the prose repair is a scope decision and it belongs to a person, and the ten chapters are evidence that it can be done and not evidence that it has been done.*

**Three things a later repair of this kind should not have to learn the hard way, and they are all of them defects this phase introduced and then caught.** *One:* copying a canon set-piece from an earlier chapter verbatim raises the re-print count against a chapter that was not touched, and the fix is to take the original wording of the passage, which is the wording the volume already had, and not to paraphrase a canon figure. *Two:* **this volume's framing is third person and its interiority is first person, and the interiority section is opened by a bold line — but that is a default, not a rule, and the test that holds is narrower.** Measured over the whole manuscript, 106 chapters of 620 end their last section in first person, twenty of them in Volumes 01 and 02, and inside Volume 12 the exceptions are the closing paragraph of `chapter-0601.md` and the interiority of `chapter-0603.md`, which is not closed by a section break. **A repair that states the rule absolutely will teach the next phase a rule the untouched chapters contradict, and will also start unwinding a legitimate first-person close.** The operational test is: *keep each chapter's closing section in the person its original closed in.* *Three:* the demonstrative-anaphora defect is concentrated, and the ten named forms account for a large share of a movement's total, so a repair that measures the concentration on **its own target range and not on the range it was measured on last time** and deals with the top forms first moves the rate faster than a repair that varies sentences evenly. `that table`, `that door` and `that room` lead Chapters 0601 to 0609 and `that lane`, which led the previous ten, is all but absent from them.

**Five more, added by the pass that read the review of this phase, and none of them was caught by the phase that made them.** *Four:* **an age is a fact about a person, and this repair invented one.** `chapter-0610.md` gave the woman at the foot of that stair an age, fifty-one, and there is no age for her anywhere in the six hundred and twenty chapters; it is out, and the two women of fifty-one who do exist, in `chapter-0118.md` and `chapter-0412.md`, are different people in different rooms. **This is the second time this repository has had to confess a figure the page does not support** — the first was a child in `chapter-0620.md` whose age the state layer itself printed, and that one was caught here; this one was in a chapter file and was caught by a review. **Before a repair adds any figure, object, duration, distance or age, grep the whole of `chapters/` for it, and if the only hits are this manuscript's other, unrelated people, it is a new fact and it does not go in.** *Five:* **read the last paragraph of the chapter you opened against the last paragraph of the chapter after it, and read the chapter's own middle against its own end.** `chapter-0610.md` ended with a closing section that removed the coin and a paragraph beneath it that said the coin was on the tread, and `chapter-0611.md` opens on the eighth morning with the tread bare. Eight lines apart and across a chapter boundary, and a re-print scan, a calendar scan and a question-mark count all passed it. *Six:* **a section break is architecture and a rewrite that moves one has changed the book and not only the prose.** The original `chapter-0610.md` had four breaks; the repair left it with six, split the interiority, and pulled its material out under the new breaks — which is also what put first person into framing. All ten files are back at their original break counts. *Seven:* **print the command beside every figure, and if you cannot print the command, do not print the figure.** This phase printed word counts and anaphora rates whose classifier lived in `/tmp`, and not one of them reproduced. The rates have been replaced with a self-contained instrument printed whole in `state/batch-summary.md`, and the word counts now come from `tools/measure.py`'s own function. *Eight:* the four re-print runs reported against Volume 12 are one passage, the 0586 case-and-blanks set-piece against 0619, at four sliding offsets of one twenty-word window. **A run count is not a count of duplications, and a record that prints the first invites a reader to believe the second.**

**Three more, added by the pass that read the review of the second ten chapters, `chapter-0601.md` to `chapter-0609.md`, and all three of them are the same shape as the ones above: the pass that made the change did not notice it and the review did.** *Eight:* **a repair may not change a canon figure to make a sentence read better, however good the reason.** The second pass rewrote one word in `chapter-0602.md`, from eleven *weeks* to eleven *years*, without a line written anywhere about it, and **the word was very probably a correction** — eleven weeks appears nowhere else in Volume 12 and that chapter says eleven years twice itself. **It was still put back, because a prose repair has no authority to decide a canon question, and the tension is left standing and visible for a person to settle instead of settled on the side that reads better.** *Nine:* **one canon edit broke two rules at once, and that is the ordinary way a small mistake turns into a large one.** That single word also made `chapter-0602.md` share a twenty-three-word passage with `chapter-0617.md`, a chapter the repair never opened, which took Volume 12 from its baseline of four prose re-prints to six and broke a ceiling the prompt set explicitly. **This is the second time this repository has raised that count by rewording a canon figure to match another chapter. Check the re-prints after every canon figure and not only at the end.** *Ten:* **a repair can put the same gesture on the page twice in consecutive paragraphs and every instrument in this repository will pass it.** The second pass left `chapter-0609.md` with the woman opening the paper out of her apron and pressing it flat to the board with the heel of her hand, and the original line four lines beneath had her put the fourth item back onto that board and square it with the heel of her hand. **The re-print scan passed it, the calendar scan passed it, the question-mark count passed it and the closing-person scan passed it, and the thing that caught it was a person reading the chapter.** Item five above is the general rule and this is the same rule a second time. **The standing a later repair inherits is that a paragraph added between two original paragraphs must not perform the act either neighbour performs, and that a scene is checked by reading it and not by scanning it.**

**Nothing about this item bears on the four debts, and neither pass discharged any of them.**

## 173. THE PROSE REPAIR IS NINETEEN CHAPTERS DONE OF SIX HUNDRED AND TWENTY, THE NEXT TEN ARE NAMED, AND THE SCOPE DECISION IS STILL A PERSON'S

*Added by the pass that took `workspace/prose-repair-0002/PROMPT.md` and then read `logs/prose-repair-0002.review.log`. It is a repair and not a batch. It planned no volume, wrote no chapter, and `chapter-0620.md` was not opened.*

**Nineteen chapters of six hundred and twenty have now been through this repair and they are `chapter-0610.md` to `chapter-0619.md` and `chapter-0601.md` to `chapter-0609.md`.** That is three per cent of the manuscript and eight of the seventy chapters in the volume where the defect is worst. The nineteen together stand at 25,395 words and 104 hits of the ten named forms, **4.10 per 1,000**, against Volume 01's 0.29 and Volume 12's 6.98 as it now stands; the range just repaired is at 1.52 and the one before it at 6.85, and **Volume 12 came down by 1.45 per thousand across the nine chapters of this pass.** **The measured record for the second range is in `state/batch-summary.md` under the heading for it, with the method printed whole beside every figure, and it is not restated here.** Item 170 remains open. Nothing in either pass closed it.

**What is left is most of the manuscript, and the honest way to say the size of it is in phases and not in chapters.** Volumes 08, 09, 10 and 11 and the first fifty chapters of Volume 12 carry the same defect and none of them has been touched. **Six hundred and one chapters are unrepaired and a repair continuing ten chapters at a time is sixty-one phases; restricted to the four volumes where the collapse is worst and the untouched half of Volume 12 it is two hundred and fifty chapters, which is twenty-five phases.** *This is the honest shape of the item and it is the standing the next phase inherits: the prose repair is a scope decision and it belongs to a person, and the ten chapters are evidence that it can be done and not evidence that it has been done.*

**The next ten chapters are named, so that the next phase does not have to choose and cannot choose wrong.** They are **`chapter-0591.md` through `chapter-0600.md`**, the ten immediately before the range just repaired, the last ten chapters of Movement Five, and untouched by any repair. **The reason for that choice is the same one that chose 0610 to 0619 before it and it is the concentration, not the order. Measure the concentration on the range you are opening before you open it, and deal with the top forms on that range first.** `chapter-0620.md` is not in that range, is not to be opened, and carries the last line of this series.

**And the next phase must measure those ten rather than inherit a list, because their leaders are not the closed list's and this pass measured them.** On the instrument printed in `state/batch-summary.md`, `chapter-0591.md` to `chapter-0600.md` stands at 9,677 words and 85 hits of the ten named forms, 8.78 per 1,000. Its leaders are `that floor` 18, `that room` 14, `that passage` 12, `those boards` 9, `that door` 9, `that table` 7, `that stair` 7, `that tin` 5, `that lane` 4 — **and `that passage`, `those boards` and `that lane` are near the bottom of what they are on the range just repaired, so a phase that reuses that range's table and stops at `that door`, `that floor` and `that room` will leave the leaders of the new range standing.** A wider sweep of every demonstrative plus noun in those ten files, which is the sweep a closed list cannot do, puts **`that end` 13, `that landing` 13, `that bench` 12, `that sill` 10, `that corridor` 8 and `that case` 8** in or near the lead, and **not one of those six is in the closed list of ten forms that this repository has been measuring with since the first repair.** That is the same blind spot the instrument's own note admits and it is now measured on a range rather than reasoned about. **A closed list measures the concentration a repair worked on and not the whole of item 170, and a phase that widens the list should say so in its record and print the old and the new rate side by side.**

**And four findings the review of this range hands the next phase, all of them in `state/batch-summary.md` under its section Nine and none of them new plot.**

*Thirty-six, and it is the instrument.* **The standing that a twelve-token index is the import check is withdrawn and replaced by a command: `python3 tools/measure.py lifts --volume 12 --first 561 --last 570 --base $BASE --min 6 --show 20`, which returns for each added prose line the longest run of words standing contiguously in one single other chapter, indexed over the chapters outside the range, and prints the volume's own formula baseline beside the added figure. It carries two plants in `selftest`. On this range the baseline was **204 original prose lines, 179 over six words, 105 over nine, mean 9.45**, and the added prose **63 lines, 61 over six, 14 over nine, mean 7.74** — below the prose it was set beside. **Read the added figure against the baseline and do not revoice on the added figure alone; what is real is the re-narration of a fixture the chapter's own room has already settled, and reading is what finds that.**

*Thirty-seven, and it is the window.* **A re-print figure is meaningless without its window, and Volume 12 returns 4, 392, 1,699 and 4,449 prose runs at windows 20, 12, 8 and 5.** The standing that *the baseline is four and do not go above four* is withdrawn: it is a statement about a twenty-word window, and at twelve words the same volume reports three hundred and ninety-two. **Run all four windows.** And **the `terms` classifier's twelve prose runs at window twenty are the same four plus eight date lines**, the longest being the calendar sentence in `chapter-0567.md`, which is inside the range the next phase is about to open. **Neither classifier is wrong; print all three buckets and read the runs.**

*Thirty-eight, and it is a citation.* **A line number is a citation of an original line in a file this repair did not touch, and of nothing else.** This item's own four canon conflicts are on files no pass has edited, which is why their ten numbers still resolve; the four the record of this range added beside them were pre-repair numbers on files that pass had opened, and all four were wrong — `chapter-0577.md:47` for `:59`, `chapter-0571.md:25` for `:33`, `chapter-0575.md:11` for `:13`, `chapter-0573.md:39` for `:49` — printed four lines below a sentence asserting that every cited line number had been checked. **In a file this repair has edited, cite the quoted text and the chapter.**

*Thirty-nine, and it is a claim about a removal.* **When a pass reports an instance out, check first that the instance is one the pass wrote.** This item's record reported removing *about that room* from `chapter-0577.md:63`; it is **original, it is at `e6f2bb1:49`, and it is still on the page.** No count was ever wrong because of it. **A record that claims a fix it did not make is worse than a record that is silent, because the next pass reads it as a cleared field.**

**Four debts are outstanding and neither pass touched them, and they are owed elsewhere and not here.** **Nothing about this item bears on the four debts and neither pass discharged any of them.**


## 173A. THE PROSE REPAIR IS TWENTY-NINE CHAPTERS DONE OF SIX HUNDRED AND TWENTY, THE NEXT TEN ARE `chapter-0581.md` TO `chapter-0590.md`, AND THE SCOPE DECISION IS STILL A PERSON'S

*Added by the pass that took `workspace/prose-repair-0003/PROMPT.md`. It is a repair and not a batch, not a close, not a review and not an outline phase. It planned no volume, wrote no chapter, and `chapter-0620.md` was not opened. **Item 170 remains open. Nothing in this pass closed it.***

**Twenty-nine chapters of six hundred and twenty had been through the prose repair at the time of this item: `chapter-0610.md` to `chapter-0619.md`, `chapter-0601.md` to `chapter-0609.md`, and `chapter-0591.md` to `chapter-0600.md`. That is four and a half per cent of the manuscript and twenty-nine of the seventy chapters in the volume where the defect is worst. **Volumes 08, 09, 10 and 11 were untouched in their entirety and the first forty chapters of Volume 12 were untouched.** Five hundred and ninety-one chapters were unrepaired; a repair continuing in this shape at ten chapters a phase is fifty-nine phases, and restricted to the four volumes where the collapse is worst and the untouched forty chapters of Volume 12 it is two hundred and forty chapters, which is twenty-four phases. **This is the standing and it has now moved: item 173D has since taken `chapter-0581.md` to `chapter-0590.md`, and the current standing is thirty-nine chapters and six and a quarter per cent, printed at 173D and not here. The prose repair is a scope decision and it belongs to a person, and no phase may imply the job is nearly done.** Method: `python3 -c "import glob; print(len(glob.glob('chapters/volume-*/*.md')))"` for 620, and the same over `chapters/volume-{08,09,10,11}/*.md` for 200.

**The measured record for the ten just repaired is in `state/batch-summary.md` under the heading *THE PROSE REPAIR OF CHAPTERS 0591 TO 0600*, with the method printed whole beside every figure, and it is not restated here.** The three figures that belonged in this file and nowhere else were the ones a later phase needed to know which range it was standing next to, and as of item 173A they were **the twenty-nine repaired chapters of Volume 12 at 39,352 words, at 4.24 per 1,000 on the closed list of ten named forms and 9.38 on the widened list of twenty-three; the forty untouched chapters from 0551 to 0590 at 42,538 words, a mean of 1,063 a chapter, at 8.23 and 15.49. Both are superseded — item 173D repaired `chapter-0581.md` to `chapter-0590.md`, the untouched range is now the thirty chapters from 0551 to 0580, and the current figures are at 173D and in `state/current.md`. Do not read either pair above as the standing.** Repaired prose and unrepaired prose are two different textures sitting in one volume.

**Three things this pass adds, and the first is the most expensive defect this repair has produced.**

*Eleven, and it belongs beside item five.* **A repair can put a beat into a chapter that belongs to a chapter six files later, and no instrument in this repository can see it.** The first draft of `chapter-0600.md` had the courier turn round for the first time in eleven years. **`chapter-0606.md` owns that beat in three places and `chapter-0609.md` then has him not turning round again; the repair had taken Volume 12's climax and the whole of its consequence six chapters early.** A re-print scan, a calendar scan, a question-mark count, a closing-person scan, a date-line check and a section-break check all passed the file with the error standing in it. It was found by **reading `chapter-0606.md` to find out whether the knife is in the drawer that is shut, for an unrelated reason.** The standing that comes out of it is the generalisation of item five and it is now confirmed at the worst available price: **a chapter has to be read against the chapters that own the events it is near, and those are not the chapters either side of it.** A repair that reads only its own range and its immediate neighbours is reading a tenth of what it needs to read.

*Twelve, and it belongs beside item nine, with the direction reversed.* **The first draft lifted four canon set-pieces word for word out of other chapters of the same volume** — 62 words from 0606 into 0600, 37 and 21 from 0601 into 0594, 24 from 0606 into 0598, 23 and 20 from 0581 and 0584 into 0591 — and `python3 tools/measure.py reprints --window 20 --volume 12` went from **4 to 16**, over a ceiling the prompt set at 4. Item nine is a canon figure reworded *to match* another chapter; this is another chapter's wording imported *into* this one, and the effect on the count is the same and larger. **Then the repair made the same fault inside its own range and had to fix it a second time.** The fix both times is the same: **take the volume's own facts and write them out in fresh words, and never copy a sentence out of a chapter, including out of one the repair is not opening.** **A closed list of figures and objects is not a licence to lift a sentence, and the re-print instrument is the only thing here that catches either direction.**

*Thirteen, and it belongs beside item four, which now has two instances rather than one.* **An age is a fact about a person and so is a room's furniture, and a repair that greps the whole of `chapters/` before it adds a detail finds that most of what it wants to describe is already on the page in the same room.** Set dressing for these ten rooms already existed in `chapter-0581.md`, `0584.md`, `0585.md`, `0569.md`, `0601.md`, `0605.md` and `0606.md` — the sill about four feet long, the bench the man of about sixty-one reaches it from without standing up, the daylight over a wall about five foot high, the tin with two pen that are the same pen, three keys on a board, boards soft at the edges and hard in the middle, skirting painted with the paint gone at the height of a cloth, a case with a lid that goes up at the back, a board that is soft and dark with four things on it and four taken off and no line to them. **This pass drafted and then took out a groove in the near edge of a case, a second pen and a glass on a clerk's table, a mat down the middle of a passage, a lock and two handles on a tin, a fourth bare hook on a board by a front door, a planked table on two trestles, a high window in a shed, a chain on a wheel, a cobbled lane with a kerb and a grating, a chair in a room at the back of a floor, and a claim that the woman from the landing filled a pail. Two of those were flatly contradicted by the page: a planked trestle table in the man of thirty-eight's room, where `chapter-0601.md` and `chapter-0619.md` have it deal with its far edge into the wall; and a high window in the shed, where `chapter-0569.md` has the light coming off the roof.** The standing is the one item four already set and it is now wider than figures and ages: **it is any fact about the world, and the test is the same, which is that the only hits must not be this manuscript's other and unrelated people or rooms.**

**The next ten chapters are named, so that the next phase does not have to choose and cannot choose wrong.** They are **`chapter-0581.md` through `chapter-0590.md`**, Movement Four, the ten immediately before the range just repaired, and untouched by any repair. **The choice is the same one that has chosen this range twice and it is the concentration, not the order: measure the concentration on the range you are opening before you open it, and deal with the top forms on that range first.**

**And the next phase must measure those ten rather than inherit a list, because their leaders are neither this range's leaders nor the previous range's, and this pass measured them.** On the same two instruments, `chapter-0581.md` to `chapter-0590.md` stands at **9,925 words, 106 hits of the closed list of ten, 10.68 per 1,000** — the highest rate of any of the three repaired ranges before repair, against this one's 8.78 and the 0601 to 0609 range's 11.18. **Its closed-list leaders are `that floor` 34, `that room` 24, `those boards` 14, `that door` 12 and `that stair` 10, and they are led by a single form at thirty-four.** The wider sweep of every demonstrative plus noun in those ten files, with the function-word tail excluded, returns **274 hits in 9,925 words, 27.61 per 1,000, across a form list whose leaders include `that sill` 31, `that building` 17, `that corridor` 14, `that end` 11, `that board` 11, `that stone` 7, `that ground` 7 and `that house` 7** — **and `that sill`, `that building`, `that board`, `that stone` and `that house` are not in the closed list of ten and four of the five are not in the widened list of twenty-three either.** `that building` at seventeen is a form this repository has never counted in any list. **A closed list measures the concentration a repair worked on and not the whole of item 170, and a phase that widens the list should say so in its record and print the old and the new rate side by side.** The method for both sweeps is printed whole in `state/batch-summary.md` in the section headed *THE PROSE REPAIR OF CHAPTERS 0591 TO 0600* and should be pasted rather than rebuilt.

**Four debts are outstanding and no pass in this repair touched them, and they are owed elsewhere and not here.**
