## 173B. A REPAIR PHASE CAN BE DISPATCHED TWICE, THE SECOND DISPATCH CANNOT TELL FROM THE REPOSITORY THAT THE WORK IS DONE, AND WHAT THE RE-AUDIT FOUND WHEN IT CHECKED

*Added by a phase handed `workspace/prose-repair-0003/PROMPT.md` a second time, the same prompt `0efc9c8` had already taken and committed. It planned no volume, wrote no chapter, added nothing to any plot, and did not open `chapter-0620.md`. **Item 170 remains open. Nothing in this pass closed it, and this pass repaired no new chapters.** It read the ten chapters its prompt assigns, found them already repaired, and audited them instead of rewriting them.*

**The finding, and it belongs beside item 168 because it is the same class.** The range 0591 to 0600 was repaired, committed, measured and recorded before this phase opened one of the ten files. **Every figure in that committed record reproduces exactly** — 9,677 words and 85 closed-list hits in the pre-repair text and 13,957 words and 63 hits in the first pass's text, on the method printed whole in `state/batch-summary.md`. **Name the commits and not the offsets: the pre-repair text of that range is at `HEAD~3` (`2894856`) and the first pass's own text is at `HEAD~2` (`0efc9c8`); the re-audit is `71bd594` and the EPUB build at `HEAD~1` touches no chapter.** *This pairing was first printed here and in the batch summary as `HEAD~2` and `HEAD`, which is one commit out on both sides, and the review of that pass corrected it; a phase that had run the old labels would have got 13,957 where the record promised 9,677. See item 173C.* **The repository did not carry any marker saying the phase was done, so a second dispatch had no way to know but to check, and the cost of not checking is a repair that rewrites prose that is already right.** The standing: **a prose-repair phase must compare its range against the recorded pre-repair figures before it edits anything, and a range that is already repaired owes a re-audit and a record and not a second rewrite.**

**Eight defects the first pass introduced and its own record did not know about, all fixed here, and one regression of its own.** A fourteen-word phrase lifted out of `chapter-0603.md` into `chapter-0600.md`; a paragraph in the same file contradicting the line above it, fingers that did not lift and then travelled; an age welded onto a physical action; a four-clause set-piece carried over from `chapter-0606.md` closely enough to read as a copy; two verbatim lifts out of `chapter-0605.md`; a fourteen-word lift out of `chapter-0577.md`; an added paragraph that reassigned the arrangement at the end of `chapter-0597.md`'s bench from the boy to the foreman and took from him the credit the chapter says nobody will give him; and `chapter-0598.md` given a courier standing in two places at once. **And the first pass stripped the trailing newline from all ten files, which no instrument in this repository can see.** All nine are corrected and every figure is in `state/batch-summary.md` under the heading *THE RE-AUDIT AND SECOND REPAIR OF CHAPTERS 0591 TO 0600*.

**Four things the audit learned that belong to the next phase whatever range it opens.**

*Fourteen.* **The re-print instrument's window is the size of the blind spot in the instrument that polices imports.** `python3 tools/measure.py reprints --window 20 --volume 12` returned **4** on every state of this range, baseline and ceiling both 4, and passed a file carrying four separate imports of 12 to 17 words each. **The import class this repair declares closed is closed only above the window it measures.** A phase that wants to know whether it has copied a sentence has to look at the text and not only at the count, and **the fix in every instance was to take the volume's own facts and write them out in fresh words.**

*Fifteen.* **A chapter's own title is a line of canon and it is the one line a repair's checks never compare.** `chapter-0598.md` is titled *"A Woman At A Case Twenty Feet Off A Man…"*, and **twenty feet appears nowhere in the six hundred and twenty chapters** — it is in `chapter-0002.md` and `chapter-0387.md` about other people and other rooms. The body of the chapter says a yard. **The title is byte-identical to `HEAD~2` and it is the volume's, and this phase did not touch it and no phase may.** It is recorded here because the date-line check and the section-break check both passed a chapter whose title asserts a figure its own body contradicts. **`sed -n '1p' | md5sum` against `HEAD` is a check this repair did not run and the next one should.**

*Sixteen, and it is the standing that keeps recurring.* **Three claims in the delivered record do not hold, and the prose behind two of them is correct while the sentence certifying it is not.** *"Every line between quotation marks is the original's"* is false in `chapter-0593.md`, where one demonstrative changed inside a quoted line. *"About four hundred blanks in total in the box that is not the case at 0592, 0598 and 0600"* is false at all three, and at the two where the words appear they say *a year* and mean the case. **A record that certifies a property nobody ran a command for is a record that will be wrong, and this repository now has three of them.** The standing is unchanged and it is the one item four sets: **grep the whole of `chapters/` before you print a figure, and grep your own record's figures before you inherit them.**

*Seventeen.* **The state layer can describe a beat with a duration the page never carried, and a phase that reads only the state layer will report a canon change that did not happen.** `state/character-state.md:75` says she took her fingers off *"about half an hour later"*; `git show HEAD~3:chapters/volume-12/chapter-0600.md | grep -c 'half an hour'` returns **0**. **Read the page for what the page says, and read the state layer for what the state layer believes, and do not merge them in either direction.**

**The standing on scope was unchanged by this pass, and item 173D has since moved it to thirty-nine chapters.** At the time of this item: twenty-nine chapters of six hundred and twenty, five hundred and ninety-one unrepaired, Volumes 08 to 11 at two hundred chapters untouched, fifty-nine phases at ten chapters and twenty-four restricted to the four volumes where the collapse is worst. **Ten chapters is a tenth of one volume against a defect that spans four volumes, and the decision to continue, to widen, or to stop is a person's.** **The next ten chapters remain `chapter-0581.md` through `chapter-0590.md`, and the measurement of their concentration, printed at item 173A above, stands: 10.68 per 1,000 on the closed list of ten, led by `that floor` at thirty-four, with `that building` at seventeen a form no list in this repository has ever counted.**

## 173C. THE RE-AUDIT OF 0591 TO 0600 WAS REVIEWED, THREE OF ITS OWN EDITS WERE WRONG, AND THREE OF ITS FIGURES WERE LABELLED AT THE WRONG COMMIT

*Added by a phase handed `logs/prose-repair-0003.review.log`, the review of the re-audit at item 173B. **Item 170 remains open. Nothing in this pass closed it, this pass repaired no new chapters, and `chapter-0620.md` was not opened.** Three chapters were edited: `chapter-0597.md`, `chapter-0598.md`, `chapter-0600.md`. The eight defects the re-audit found are still fixed. The full record is in `state/batch-summary.md` under the heading *THE REVIEW FIXES ON CHAPTERS 0591 TO 0600*.

**Three prose defects the re-audit left standing, all fixed, and one of them is the class this repair keeps finding.**

*Eighteen, and it is the one a reader would have caught.* **`chapter-0597.md` said a month of mornings and one sentence in the same paragraph.** The re-audit removed *"she put it there"* and left *"it has been in use at the far end of it every working morning for about a month … and she is not going to say a second one out loud in that bay"*, in a chapter whose interiority opens with *"I said one true thing out loud in this bay in the third week of the third month,"* dated the fourth day of the third week of the third month. **It now reads that it has been used once, in the third week of the third month.** Two chapters this repair never opened settle it: `chapter-0602.md:33` — *"I put one true sentence out loud into this bay in the third week of the third month"* — and `chapter-0573.md:49`. **The same sentence also carried *"neither of them"* for a pair that does not exist; the bay holds three people and the phrase had lost its referent when the clause it belonged to was cut. Both are out.** The phase's own record reported that sentence as clean, which is why it is written down here: **a record that says a sentence is clean has checked the wording and not the arithmetic of the chapter.**

*Twenty.* **`chapter-0598.md`'s interiority had a demonstrative restored without anything for it to point at.** Putting *"that end of this room"* back was correct, because `:11` is original and puts him *"at the near end of the boards a yard off the case"* — **but the re-audit did not notice that `:29` stands alone in its paragraph in a chapter running four ends**, and it now reads *"at the case end of this room"*, which is true on the page, is her own dialogue at `:17` — *"There is a case at this end of these boards"* — and costs nothing in length. **The standing this confirms, and it is the record's own: a demonstrative cannot be restored without restoring what it points at. And the same chapter's three-way positional tension — body says a yard, interiority said an unanchored end, title says twenty feet — was recorded for the title and not for the interiority, which is the inconsistent application of a standing inside one file.**

*Twenty-one.* **`chapter-0600.md` said one fact twice in consecutive paragraphs and turned a position into a sequence.** *"She is the oldest of the three that work at it"* at `:9` and *"she is the oldest of the three that work at that end of the floor"* at `:11`, the second folded in by the fix for an age welded to a physical action; the duplicate is out and the canon age of thirty-four stays. And *"the fourth went on in between the other two"* made a sequence of a position, in a paragraph that has just said nobody ever asked the board *"which order they went on in"* — **it is now *"the fourth is in between the other two"*, which is what the original said and what `chapter-0606.md:11` says.**

**Three record claims that did not hold, corrected in place and left findable, and they are all one mistake wearing three coats: a figure read off the wrong reference.**

*Twenty-two, and it is the expensive one.* **The re-audit's reproduction recipe was one commit out on both sides.** It labelled 9,677 words *"before, at `HEAD~2`"* and 13,957 *"as saved, at `HEAD`"* and told the next phase to run the printed script with those two in place of its before and after. Reproduced: **`HEAD~3` = 9,677, `HEAD~2` = 13,957, `HEAD` = 13,938.** **The figures were never wrong and the labels were, and the labels are what the next phase runs.** Corrected in the batch summary's sections One, Five and Six and in item 173B, with the four commits now named in the record: `2894856` pre-repair, `0efc9c8` the first pass, `5769967` the EPUB build, `71bd594` the re-audit.

*Twenty-three.* **The per-chapter before-to-now column was wrong in six of ten rows and mixed two references**, summing to 13,936 against the 13,938 in the row above it. **The tell is a column that does not sum to the total printed beside it, and a repair should check that before it publishes it.**

*Twenty-four.* **The trailing-newline section asserted its own shell test backwards** — `[ -n "$(tail -c 1 f)" ]` is false for a file ending in a newline, so true means *missing*, and the section said true of the working tree and false at `HEAD~2` when it is the other way about. **Three records in this repair have now asserted a command's result instead of running it, and all three were wrong in the same direction: the writer reported the state it had produced rather than the state it had found.**

**The standing this adds, and it is short. Name the commits, not the offsets.** A repair's *before* is the commit that held the chapters before the repair, and **the distance between that commit and the working tree is not knowable from the working tree** — `5769967` sits between the first pass and the re-audit and touches no chapter, which is exactly the kind of commit that turns `HEAD~2` into a moving target. Write `2894856` beside the figure and the three errors above are impossible. **And check that a column of figures sums to the total printed beside it.**

**What was checked after these three edits, with the command beside each figure.** `measure.py selftest` **PASS on all nine plants**; `calendar --volume 12` seventy files read, seventy date lines parsed, twelve restarts, **zero breaks inside a month**, unparsed none; `reprints --window 20 --volume 12` **4 prose runs against a baseline and ceiling of 4**, longest 24 words in `chapter-0614.md`, all in files this pass did not open; `markers` over the ten files **zero files with an odd count**. **Date lines and titles ten of ten byte-identical to `HEAD~3`, section breaks 5/4/4/4/5/4/4/4/3/4/4 all matching, question marks zero, month and weekday names zero, `this year`/`this volume`/`this novel` zero, trailing newlines ten of ten present, `chapter-0620.md` untouched. Closing-section person against `HEAD~3`: zero mismatches, all ten THIRD.** Speech, `re.findall(r'"[^"]*"')` per file against `HEAD~3`: **nine IDENTICAL and one CHANGED**, `chapter-0593.md`, which is item 173B's finding nine and not a change of this pass — **none of the three edits made here is inside quotation marks.** Imports at the size no instrument sees: for each changed line **the longest run occurring anywhere in the manuscript is twenty-four words and is held by its own chapter alone, and no run of six words or more of the new wording occurs in any other chapter.** The range now stands at **13,926 words, closed-ten 64 = 4.60 per 1,000, widened-23 136 = 9.77 per 1,000, mean 1,393 a chapter**; Volume 12 is 83,217 words and the manuscript 1,393,138 in 620 files.

**The standing on scope was unchanged by this pass and item 173D has since moved it to thirty-nine chapters.** Twenty-nine chapters of six hundred and twenty repaired, five hundred and ninety-one unrepaired, Volumes 08 to 11 untouched at two hundred chapters, and the scope decision is still a person's. **The next ten chapters remain `chapter-0581.md` through `chapter-0590.md` at 9,925 words, 106 closed-list hits, 10.68 per 1,000, per chapter 1,117 1,040 1,094 1,037 986 974 928 882 905 962** — and **those recorded figures are the precheck, because a comparison of the working tree against `HEAD` at phase start is a comparison of a tree with itself.**

## 173D. THE PROSE REPAIR IS THIRTY-NINE CHAPTERS DONE OF SIX HUNDRED AND TWENTY, THE NEXT TEN ARE `chapter-0571.md` TO `chapter-0580.md`, AND THREE CANON CONFLICTS ARE CARRIED UNSETTLED

*Added by the phase that took `workspace/prose-repair-0004/PROMPT.md`. **It is a repair and not a batch, not a close, not a review and not an outline phase.** It planned no volume, wrote no chapter, and `chapter-0620.md` was not opened. **Item 170 remains open. Nothing in this pass closed it.** The full measured record is in `state/batch-summary.md` under the heading *THE PROSE REPAIR OF CHAPTERS 0581 TO 0590*, with the method printed whole beside every figure, and it is not restated here.*

**Thirty-nine chapters of six hundred and twenty have now been through the prose repair: `chapter-0610.md` to `chapter-0619.md`, `chapter-0601.md` to `chapter-0609.md`, `chapter-0591.md` to `chapter-0600.md`, and `chapter-0581.md` to `chapter-0590.md`.** That is six and a quarter per cent of the manuscript and thirty-nine of the seventy chapters in the volume where the defect is worst. **Volumes 08, 09, 10 and 11 are untouched in their entirety and the first thirty chapters of Volume 12 are untouched.** Five hundred and eighty-one chapters are unrepaired; a repair continuing in this shape at ten chapters a phase is fifty-eight phases, and restricted to the four volumes where the collapse is worst and the untouched thirty chapters of Volume 12 it is two hundred and thirty chapters, which is twenty-three phases. Method: `python3 -c "import glob; print(len(glob.glob('chapters/volume-*/*.md')))"` for 620, and the same over `chapters/volume-{08,09,10,11}/*.md` for 200. **This is the standing and it has not moved: the prose repair is a scope decision and it belongs to a person, and thirty-nine chapters are evidence that it can be done and not evidence that it has been done. No phase may imply the job is nearly done.**

**The one figure in this item that a later phase needs is the one that says what the repair actually did, and it is two numbers, not one.** Over the ten chapters just repaired, **the sentences the original wrote still run at 10.71 closed-list hits per 1,000 words and did not move, because not one word of them was touched, and the 5,220 words this repair wrote run at 2.49 per 1,000, a quarter of that.** The file-level rate fell from 10.68 to 7.91 and every absolute count went up, because the prose grew fifty-two per cent and the counts did not. **A repair that lengthens prose without cutting the construction lowers the rate and raises the count at the same time, and the count is the one a reader feels, so a later repair should print both and should not be satisfied with a rate that fell only because the denominator grew.** The full tables are in the batch summary and the standing this adds is item 173D's fourth paragraph.

**Three canon conflicts this repair found and did not settle, and a prose repair has no authority to settle any of them. All three are pre-existing; none was introduced; none is on a page this pass wrote.**

*Twenty-five, and it is a chapter that disagrees with itself.* **`chapter-0590.md:9` says the door at the back of that floor "has been shut for a fortnight" and `chapter-0590.md:41` says that same door "stood open about as long as it takes to square a sheet".** Both sentences are original and both stand, and this pass added nothing to either. **The chapter's own interiority and its own closing section both say the door has not been opened, and `chapter-0589.md`, a week earlier, has the same door shut the whole of the rest of that morning.** **This needs a human decision and it is the clearest instance this repair has found of a chapter that cannot be true twice in one morning.**

*Twenty-six, and it is the same figure in two mouths.* **The girl of nineteen says she gave up the only thing she had "in a month that is gone" at `chapter-0581.md:43`, dated the eleventh month, and "until about two months ago" at `chapter-0589.md:15` and `:31`, dated the first month of the next year.** `chapter-0596.md:27` says "in a month that is gone" again and `chapter-0604.md:29` says "about two months ago", so the volume states it at two durations in four chapters. **The word "about" covers part of the gap and not all of it, and the repair has left every one of the four exactly as it stands.**

*Twenty-seven, and it is an object with a date on it.* **The four inches the pot was moved along the sill is "in the last fortnight" at `chapter-0584.md:3`, dated the second day of the second week of the twelfth month, and "about a fortnight ago" at `chapter-0589.md:9`, dated the fourth day of the third week of the first month of the next year, and `chapter-0591.md:11` has about three feet of the sill empty "for a fortnight" at the second day of the first week of the second month.** Three dates, three accounts, one moved pot. **Nothing this pass wrote touches the pot, the four inches or any of the three dates.**

**And two findings about how the state layer and the page can be made to disagree, which are item 173B's finding seventeen in a new place.**

*Twenty-eight.* **A repair that writes a chapter out whole will retype the date line, and this pass retyped three of ten and got all three wrong by one *year after*.** `chapter-0587.md`, `chapter-0588.md` and `chapter-0589.md` each had a date line that came out a clause shorter than the commit's, and each was caught by `sed -n '5p' | md5sum` against `$BASE` and **restored from `git show $BASE:<file> | sed -n '5p'` rather than corrected by hand.** **A date line is copied out of the commit and never typed. *Thirty, and it is a chapter that gives the same person two sexes.* **`chapter-0577.md:59` says the person at the top of that stair is *a woman who comes four times a week to that room and has been handed a thing every week for eleven years*, and `chapter-0586.md:3`, `chapter-0588.md:13` and `chapter-0592.md:17` all make the person who goes up that stair four times a week with a satchel for eleven years a man.** The eleven years agrees and the sex does not. *Thirty-one, and it is a coin in two places.* **`chapter-0571.md:9` says that in about two years the only thing that has come through that door is a coin that goes flat under it and stops, `chapter-0571.md:33` is the man's own speech *I have put a coin under it for two years*, and `chapter-0566.md:17` has the coin in the gap under the door. `chapter-0575.md:13` says the first coin came under the door and he picked it up off the boards, dated the second day of the first week of the tenth month.** All five lines are original and all five stand.

**And two findings about method that are new and that the next phase inherits.**

*Thirty-four, and it is a regression of a regression.* **A re-anchoring pass and a read-back pass are two passes, and the second one can undo the first.** This pass took its own added prose to zero on the closed list, and **then the read-back pass rewrote forty-four paragraphs and four of those rewrites put the construction back**, and they were found only because the scan was run again after the read-back and not only before it. **The measurement goes after every pass and not between two of them, and a pass that has already been measured once is not measured for the last time until the next one is done.**

Two records in this repair have now had to withdraw a figure of this kind, and neither withdrawal was a failure of a method — both were a number written before the method was.**

**Three canon conflicts this repair found and did not settle, and a prose repair has no authority to settle any of them. All three are pre-existing; none was introduced; none is on a page this pass wrote, and one was nearly made worse and was not.**

**And the third finding is about the instruments themselves, and it is the one a later phase is most likely to walk into.**

*Thirty-nine, and a review has since corrected half of it, so read this item as it stands and not as it was written.* **The four re-print windows handed to this phase by its prompt — 4, 392, 1,699 and 4,449 — were correct and they do reproduce; the claim that they did not was wrong, and the wrongness was expensive because it excused a second error.** **`tools/measure.py` was changed in `12da724`, the commit that carried the review of the previous range, and its `prose_lines` helper now selects date lines by this module's own `DATE_LINE` rather than by a `startswith("It is ")`. The record of this range printed the second figure in both its base column and its now column and concluded that the four windows were identical before and after, which was the reading of a working tree twice and not a measurement of anything. The tree now returns 4 / 393 / 1,756 / 4,728, so what this pass added to the volume's duplication is nothing at twenty words, one run at twelve words, fifty-seven at eight and two hundred and seventy-nine at five, and a twenty-word window that did not move is a good result and is not no result.** **The standing this adds over the one above: a base column is measured by archive and never by reading the tree, and a record that explains a disagreement between two tables has to be asked whether it explained it or absorbed it, because naming a changed instrument as the reason is available before anybody re-runs either table.**

Per chapter, words: **0531 2,010, 0532 2,014, 0533 1,910, 0534 2,069, 0535 2,045, 0536 1,940, 0537 1,797, 0538 1,708, 0539 1,919, 0540 1,513**, and closed-list hits **5 20 1 20 11 5 5 19 11 2**. **Its closed-list leaders are `that room` 32, `that floor` 20, `that lane` 18, `that door` 13, `that stair` 8, `those boards` 3, `that tin` 2 and `that sheet` 2, with `that table` and `that passage` almost at zero at 0 and 1.** **The wider sweep of every demonstrative-plus-noun bigram, function-word tail excluded, returns 408 hits in 18,925 words, 21.56 per 1,000, across 105 distinct forms** — the highest sweep rate and the widest set of distinct forms of any range this repair has measured — **and its leaders are `that bench` 35, `that room` 32, `that bay` 25, `that floor` 20, `that lane` 18, `that shed` 16, `that building` 15, then `that door` 13, `that hour` 13, `that house` 13 and `that morning` 12.** **`that bench` is the leader and it is a form in the widened list of twenty-three and not in the closed list of ten; `that bay` is the second-largest closed-list contributor's neighbour at 25; and `that shed` at sixteen is a form this repository has counted once before, on 0571 to 0580.** **Six of the range's eleven leaders are in the widened list of twenty-five and in neither the closed list of ten nor the head of it.** **The standing that has now held seven ranges running: measure the concentration on the range you are opening and deal with the top forms there first, because the leaders move every time, and `that bench` leading at thirty-five is half again the largest leader of the range just repaired.** A closed list of named forms measures the concentration a repair works on and not the whole of item 170.

**Two instruments for that range, both taken at `1809cd9` so a phase does not have to take them again.** `python3 tools/measure.py lifts --volume 11 --first 531 --last 540 --base 1809cd9 --min 6` prints **BASELINE 337 prose lines, 285 over six words, 161 over nine, mean 9.89** — the highest baseline of any range this repair has measured, and the added figure is zero because nothing has been added to it. **The volume's re-print profile over `git archive 1809cd9 chapters/volume-11 tools` into a scratch tree is unchanged by this pass and is printed in the batch summary's section Six: 4 / 494 / 1,668 / 3,602 `either` prose at the twenty-, twelve-, eight- and five-word windows, with the longest runs at 29 in `chapter-0530.md`, 46 in `chapter-0535.md`, 103 in `chapter-0535.md` and 109 in `chapter-0519.md`. The volume flag is `--volume 11` on every one of those commands and a phase that leaves it at 12 measures the wrong volume and gets a clean answer about the wrong files.**

**Four debts are outstanding and no pass in this repair touched them, and they are owed elsewhere and not here.**

## 173K. THE REVIEW OF THE EIGHTH RANGE, SEVEN FIGURES WRONG AND ONE BYTE WRONG, ALL FIXED, AND NOT ONE WORD OF THE PROSE CHANGED

*Added by the phase that took `logs/prose-repair-0008.review.log`. **This is a review and not a repair, not a batch, not a close, not a second reading and not an outline phase.** It planned no volume, wrote no chapter, invented no person, added nothing to any plot, resolved nothing, thanked nobody, forgave nobody and sent for nobody. **Item 170 remains open. Nothing in this review closed it, and item 173J is unchanged as to scope: seventy-eight chapters are repaired, the next ten are `chapter-0531.md` to `chapter-0540.md` in Volume 11, and the scope decision is still a person's.** `chapter-0550.md` and `chapter-0620.md` were not opened and `git diff 1809cd9` over both is empty.*

**Seven figures in the record of item 173J were wrong and one byte in one chapter was wrong, and every one of them has been corrected in place with the correction printed beside the figure rather than overwriting it silently. Not one word of prose in the range was touched, and that is the claim this review leads with, because it is the one a repair of a repair most easily breaks.**

### The one chapter that changed, and it is a newline

**`chapter-0542.md` ended on two newlines and the file at `1809cd9` ends on one.** The extra byte is gone. **The chapter's word count is unchanged at 1,894, its zero `replace` opcodes and zero deleted words are unchanged, its section breaks and its last sentence are unchanged, and no reader of this series can see it.** What returned it was `od -c` on the last three lines of a file, which is not a command this repository prints anywhere: **`sed -n '5p' | md5sum` reads the fifth line, `git diff --stat` counts changed lines, and neither can see the end of a file, so a repair that only ever diffs has no way of learning that it changed the shape of a file it did not mean to change.** **This is the first byte-level defect this repair has produced and it passed all seven of the printed instruments on the range.** Method: a length of trailing `b"\n"` counted for the ten files at `1809cd9` and in the working tree returns 1 and 1 for all ten after the fix, and 1 and 2 for `chapter-0542.md` before it.

### The seven figures

| Where | Printed | Measured | What it was |
|---|---|---|---|
| batch summary, section Two | mean **1,994** for the nine opened chapters | **1,823.9** (`16,415 ÷ 9`) | a transcription slip; the ten-chapter mean of 1,793 in the table above it is right, and the nine-chapter figure was never derived from it |
| batch summary, section Three, and item 173J | sweep **258 to 266**, a rise of **eight**, rate **14.83** | **258 to 267**, a rise of **nine**, rate **14.89** | one instance short. The nine are the eight recorded inside added prose and a ninth, `that on` in `chapter-0542.md`, which is a join across a paragraph boundary and not an instance of anything. **258 plus 8 is 266, so the page carried a total the rows above it could not reach, and that is what forced it into the open** |
| batch summary, section Six, re-print table | base column headed `either` carrying **1,672** and **3,643** | `either` base **1,668** and **3,602**; 1,672 and 3,643 are the **`date`** classifier | a base read off the wrong column of a three-classifier printout. Item 173H found a base column that was the working tree measured twice; this is the same class with a different mistake inside it |
| batch summary, section Six | the pass moved the eight- and five-word counts by **+27** and **+141**, and one eight-word run per **163** added words | **+31** and **+182**, and one per **142** | computed from the wrong base above, and carried forward rather than recomputed |
| batch summary, section Six, re-print table | `terms` at 8 words **40 / 40**, `date` at 5 words **46 / 46**, `terms` at 5 words **70 / 70** | **38 / 40**, **46 / 45**, **60 / 70** | three cells miscounted on one side each |
| batch summary, sections Zero and Six, and item 173J | every date line stands on the line number it stood on at `1809cd9`, being 9, 5, 9, 9, 9, 5, 7, 11, 5, 5 | the **first** `It is ` line does, in all ten; the pattern matches twice in `chapter-0542.md`, `chapter-0544.md` and `chapter-0545.md`, and the second match moved in two — `chapter-0544.md` 15 to 17, `chapter-0545.md` 43 to 53 | the check took the first match and reported the pattern as clean, which is the defect item 173J had itself written a standing about two paragraphs earlier and then committed. **`grep "^It is "` is not a date-line check; the selector is `tools/measure.py`'s `DATE_LINE_ORD`, which wants the whole ordinal formula, and the two extra matches in this range are ordinary sentences about a tin and about nineteen years of minutes. Across Volume 11 the two patterns differ in eight files, 58 lines against 50.** |
| batch summary, section Six | the pasted `lifts` block reads `BASELINE … mean 9.89` | **9.60** | 9.89 is `chapter-0531.md` to `chapter-0540.md`'s baseline, the range item 173J names next, and it was carried into this range's block from having been read |

**Two counts were wrong in the same way and are worth naming together, because they are the cheapest class here and neither touched a chapter.** *Six of the ten files have no `**` at all* and the record said five, with the ten numbers printed beside it carrying six zeros. And the date-line paragraph reported ten numbers for a pattern that matches thirteen times. **In both cases the figure is a count of something sitting in the sentence next to it, and in both cases it was read off the sentence rather than counted by the command that produced the list.**

### What the review confirmed, and the clean list is as long as the findings

**Method: base `1809cd9`, working tree for the now, and every figure below was taken before this item was written.** `python3 tools/measure.py selftest`: **PASS on all eleven plants**. `calendar --volume 11`: **fifty files read, fifty parsed, twelve restarts, one break in month 3 with days [4, 4, 2, 2, 4, 4]** — identical to the figure the repair took. `calendar --volume 12`: **seventy files, seventy parsed, twelve restarts, zero breaks.** `words --volume 11`: **81,156**, and `words`: **1,416,184 in 620 files** — both as recorded. `git diff 1809cd9 -- chapters/volume-11/chapter-0550.md chapters/volume-12/chapter-0620.md`: **empty.**

**The claims that held, and they are the ones that matter.** **Not one original word was removed or substituted in any of the ten: zero deleted runs and zero substitutions at word level, and zero `replace` opcodes at line level**, in 59 insertions and 69 `equal` on both levels — which is the check item 173G added after a repair lost four words and every other instrument passed the file. **The three named lists are unmoved form by form: 67, 93 and 131 before and after.** Section breaks **5/5 5/5 4/4 5/5 3/3 4/4 4/4 3/3 4/4 4/4**. Speech **ten files IDENTICAL, ninety-eight quoted spans** — 19, 17, 9, 7, 5, 19, 15, 6, 1 and 0. Question marks **zero**. Month and weekday names **zero**. `this year`, `this volume`, `this novel` **zero**. Line 5 byte-identical to `1809cd9` in all ten. **`chapter-0550.md` and `chapter-0620.md` untouched.**

**The two regressions item 173J said it fixed are fixed.** The added paragraph in `chapter-0548.md` that had sat between two halves of a live exchange now stands after the last line of that exchange and before the clerk writes the day's line, which is where the record says it is and which reading the file confirms. The two sentences once appended to the end of an original line in `chapter-0541.md` are a paragraph of their own, and the zero `replace` opcodes are the evidence.

**Six of the seven figures were wrong in the record and not in the chapters, and the one that was wrong in a chapter was a newline.** That is the shape of this review and it is the shape item 173H found on the sixth range and item 173E on the fourth: **a prose repair in this repository gets the chapters approximately right and its own record approximately wrong, and the record is what the next phase reads.**

### Four standings this review adds

- **A pasted output block is transcribed, not remembered.** The `mean` on a `BASELINE` line belongs to the range whose command was run, and it has to be re-run when the range changes even when the surrounding prose does not.
- **When a command prints three classifiers over one set of runs, take the base and the now from the same classifier's row, and recompute the difference from those two figures rather than carrying an earlier reading of the same block forward.**
- **A check that takes the first match of a pattern and reports the pattern as clean has not checked the second match.** On any range where the pattern can match more than once, print every match and its line number, both sides of the change.
- **Diffing is not byte-checking.** `sed -n '5p'` reads the fifth line and `git diff --stat` counts lines; neither can see the end of a file, so the shape of a file has to be checked by a command that reads its last bytes.

**None of these four is about prose and none of them can be discharged by writing a better sentence. They are the standing for whoever repairs `chapter-0531.md` to `chapter-0540.md` next, and item 173J's own instruments for that range still stand: 18,925 words and 99 closed-list hits at 5.23 per 1,000, a 337-line baseline at mean 9.89, and a re-print profile of 4 / 494 / 1,668 / 3,602 — which, after this review, a phase can take without re-deriving them, because this review re-took all three.**

## 173L. A REPAIR PHASE WAS DISPATCHED TWICE ON CHAPTERS 0551 TO 0560, THE SECOND DISPATCH FOUND THE WORK DONE AND AUDITED IT, AND IT FOUND EIGHT DEFECTS IN A COMPLETED REPAIR — the largest single share of a repair this repository has found in finished prose

*Added by the phase handed `workspace/prose-repair-0007/PROMPT.md` a second time. **This is a re-audit and not a repair, not a batch, not a close, not a review, not a second reading and not an outline phase.** It planned no volume, wrote no chapter, invented no person, added nothing to any plot, resolved nothing, thanked nobody, forgave nobody and sent for nobody. **Item 170 remains open. Nothing in this pass closed it, and this pass repaired no new chapters: it audited ten that had been repaired at `4922b4b`.** `chapter-0620.md` and `chapter-0550.md` were not opened. **Item 173K is unchanged as to scope: seventy-eight chapters are repaired, the next ten are `chapter-0531.md` to `chapter-0540.md` in Volume 11, and the scope decision is still a person's.** The full record is in `state/batch-summary.md` under the heading *THE RE-AUDIT OF CHAPTERS 0551 TO 0560*.

**The precheck worked and it is the standing.** `$BASE` was `8ff8686a4ee56fc0840d7dbab1f191ee46b92280` and the precheck returned **16,172 words against the recorded pre-repair 11,864**, so the range was repaired and this phase owed an audit and not a rewrite. `git log --oneline -- chapters/volume-12/chapter-055[1-9].md chapters/volume-12/chapter-0560.md` names the repair at **`4922b4b`**, whose diff is 114 insertions and no deletion. **The comparison the prompt warns against was not made: at phase start the working tree and `HEAD` are the same tree by construction.** **And the marker that would have told a third dispatch this is now on disk — `workspace/prose-repair-0007/.done` — because the directory held `.attempts` and did not hold `.done` while `workspace/prose-repair-0008/` held `.done`. This is item 173B's finding, and it is the second time this repository has run it.**

### The eight defects, and they are not new plot and not a state error

**Every one is a measurable repetition, and every fix kept the fact and changed the sentence.**

- **The stone bench in the shed on the Slade is set down in the same words three times in one movement, and all three settings-down are prose `4922b4b` wrote** — in `chapter-0551.md`, `chapter-0555.md` and `chapter-0559.md`, sharing an eleven-word run, a ten-word run and a seven-word run. The canon for the fixture already sits in `chapter-0563.md` and `chapter-0597.md`, neither of which this range opened. Each chapter needs its own fact, so nothing was cut; the frames, the colour and the light are now stated three different ways.
- **A sentence the committed record claimed it had removed is still on the page.** The record of `4922b4b` says it removed a line in `chapter-0560.md` that *"said the room she has not let is at the back of her own house two lines after the original had said the same."* It had not been removed; the original line and an added line two lines below it shared nine contiguous words. **This is item 173B's thirty-nine with the roles reversed — the instance was one the pass wrote, and the record said it had gone.** A record that certifies a removal it did not make is worse than a silent one, because the next phase reads it as a cleared field.
- **`chapter-0551.md` said one distance three times in three consecutive paragraphs**, twice of them in added prose. Two instances out; the original's stands alone.
- **`chapter-0557.md`'s added paragraph ended in eight words matching an original line nine paragraphs above it.** Out.
- **`chapter-0556.md`'s closing paragraph re-listed three fixtures the chapter had already set down and opened with a nine-word run out of `chapter-0566.md`.** The re-listing is out and the point of the paragraph stands.
- **`chapter-0552.md` and `chapter-0558.md` shared nine contiguous words about the weather and the paper on the shelf**, in two added paragraphs in two chapters in the same room.
- **`chapter-0553.md` and `chapter-0557.md` shared nine contiguous words about the cloth on the boards**, same class, same movement.
- **`chapter-0557.md` lifted *"a cloth is the whole of what she does in it"* out of `chapter-0362.md` in Volume 08**, where a woman of about forty goes up a passage with a scuttle and a lamp. Three chapters carry the phrase and this is the only one where it is on a different person four volumes away.

### The instrument finding, and it is the transferable part of this phase

**`python3 tools/measure.py lifts` excludes the range under repair from its own index, and cannot return a run shared between two chapters of that range.** `tools/measure.py:442` reads `others = [p for _v, p in chapter_files() if p not in set(targets)]`, and the command prints the exclusion on every run. **On this range that blind spot held twenty-three runs of six words or more between two chapters of the range, five of them nine words or more and the longest eleven words, and the command returned none of them.** Five of the eight defects above were invisible to it. **The standing, which is for every range this repair still has to do: when `lifts` is the import check, run a second pass whose index includes the range — every added prose line against every other added prose line in the range — and treat a six-word hit as something to read rather than as a number to accept.** Method: tokenise both lines with `m.TOKEN`, lower-case, longest contiguous run in the same order in both.

**What that pass measured, before and after, and this is the cleanest figure in the phase:**

| Added prose, runs of 6+ words between two chapters of 0551 to 0560 | Runs | Longest | At 9+ |
|---|---|---|---|
| as committed at `4922b4b` | **23** | **11** | **5** |
| now | **19** | **7** | **0** |

**The twenty that stand are volume frames and were left standing deliberately**: `at the far end of the stone`, `and that is the whole of the`, `about the size of a hand where`, `an inch of wick in it that`, `and the weather comes over the sill`, `at the near end of the sill`, `the whole length of the passage and` — and, held by thirteen chapters of Volume 12 and eighty-seven chapters of this manuscript, `where the count is made` and `put her pen down`. **A book whose frames are shared on purpose cannot have them policed, and the standing against chasing the added figure alone is item 173's thirty-six.**

### Five regressions this phase introduced and fixed, which is a standing holding for the sixth time

**A re-audit pass and a read-back pass are two passes and the second can undo the first. This phase put a defect back inside its own corrections five separate times: `that room` twice and `that floor` once on the closed list, then `that shed`, `that window` and `that wood` on the whole sweep, then `those boards` and `that bed` again on the closed list, then two import runs between two of this phase's own paragraphs that the instrument invented in section Three below found four minutes after being written, then an eight-word run against `chapter-0247.md` created by the fix for the fourth. Seven closed-list and sweep instances in all, plus two run regressions, every one of them reached for while removing a repetition.** **The measurement went after every pass and not between two of them. The final state is 76, 122 and 144 on the three named lists, unmoved form by form, and one sweep instance of the discourse tail** (`that have`, the same class as `that takes` and `that run`). **The two previous ranges' read-back passes put four instances back each, and the mechanism is identical every time: a correction has to be measured as carefully as the prose it corrects.**

### Two things about the record's own figures

**`lifts`' baseline is not stable across a repair of another volume, and its added mean is not a figure of one range.** The committed record printed an added mean of **7.93** on this range; the same command on the same chapters now returns **7.79**, with the count of lines over nine words down from 19 to 14. **Neither figure is wrong.** `lifts` builds its index from every chapter file in the tree, `git diff --name-only 4922b4b HEAD -- chapters/` returns nine files and all nine are `chapters/volume-11/chapter-054[1-9].md`, and repairing Volume 11 moved the index underneath Volume 12's figure. **A later phase comparing an added mean against a figure printed by an earlier record has to name both trees, which is item 173's thirty-six again with a new instance of it.**

**One figure in the record of `4922b4b` did not reproduce and the one that did is the one that matters.** Every word figure, every closed-list figure and every widened-list figure on this range reproduced to the decimal against `0e41092` — 11,864 words, 76, 122, 144, 6.41, 10.28, 12.14, and a 243-hit sweep at 20.48 across 67 forms. **What did not hold was the record's claim that a particular sentence had been removed**, which is section Three, item two above.

### What this phase checked, with the command beside each figure

`selftest` **PASS on all eleven plants**, at the start and at the end. `calendar --volume 12` **seventy files, seventy parsed, twelve restarts, zero breaks inside a month, unparsed none**. **Date lines and titles ten of ten byte-identical to `0e41092`**, and **no chapter was written out whole**, so neither line was ever inside a replaced string. Section breaks **6/5/6/7/6/5/5/5/6/6**, every file at its original count. Trailing newlines ten of ten. Question marks **zero**. Month and weekday names **zero**. `this year`, `this volume`, `this novel` **zero**. Bold markers even in all ten. Speech **ten files IDENTICAL and zero CHANGED, sixteen quoted spans**. Closing-section person on the printed scan, planted five ways and behaving correctly on all five: **zero mismatches, all ten THIRD**. **A word-level diff against `0e41092` reports zero deleted runs, zero deleted words and zero substitutions, in 124 opcodes of which 57 are insertions** — and that is also the proof this phase changed no canon figure, because there is no word on these ten pages that the pre-repair text did not have.

**The re-print figures, all four windows and all three classifiers, base taken by archive from `0e41092`.** At the twenty-word window **4 base and 4 now**; at twelve **393 and 393**; at five **4,728 and 4,976**; and at eight **1,756 and 1,810, down twenty-one from the 1,831 the repair left.** **The audit removed twenty-one eight-word runs and added none, and that is the only figure on the four-window table that moved.**

**Volume 12 is 101,831 words and the manuscript is 1,416,164 in 620 files on `python3 tools/measure.py words`. The baseline is this phase's own `$BASE` `8ff8686`, which held 1,416,184; `1809cd9` held 1,411,772, which is 4,412 lower and is the figure the previous repair was measured against. The difference of twenty is exactly this phase's net word change on the range and is checked rather than asserted. This phase printed 1,416,184 as the figure at `1809cd9` in its first draft of this line, caught it by measuring all four commits instead of reasoning about which it meant, and corrected it — item 173's twenty-two, being a figure read off the wrong reference, found inside the record that was written to report one.**

### Nothing was resolved and nothing is settled here

**The count of things asked out loud in this matter is seven and did not move.** Nobody was thanked, nobody was forgiven, nobody was sent for, nothing was resolved, and the decision about whether a woman of twenty-four takes a colleague's judgment is unmade in both directions. **The question on the shelf is not picked up, not read and not approached**; the four feet, the eleven years, the name at the foot of it in her own hand and the two-foot window with no pane in it for about four years are canon and this phase described none of them anew. **Three canon conflicts are carried unsettled and none was added to: thirty-eight, the height of the chalk mark on the end wall in `chapter-0559.md` against `chapter-0563.md`; thirty-six, the movement-name disagreement between the outline and three prompts, now nine items old; and the wet and dry state of the passage boards, where `chapter-0553.md` and `chapter-0557.md` were both revoiced and neither now says.** The nine older conflicts stand as item 173F and item 173G left them. **This item cites no line number in a file it edited.**

### The next phase exists and this phase created nothing

**`workspace/prose-repair-0008/` already holds the prompt for `chapter-0541.md` to `chapter-0550.md` and is marked `.done`; that phase completed at `0e28a53`. `workspace/prose-repair-0009/` holds the prompt for `chapter-0531.md` to `chapter-0540.md`, carries its own measured concentration for that range, and is not done.** This phase's prompt instructed it to create a next phase prompt at `workspace/prose-repair-0008/`, and following that instruction would have overwritten the prompt of a completed phase and left the chain two phases out of step with itself. **Nothing was created. The standing is that a phase checks for its successor before it writes one, and the next phase is `workspace/prose-repair-0009/` and it is already there.**

**And the scope, said plainly because nothing in this phase narrows it.** Seventy-eight chapters of six hundred and twenty have been through this repair and this one audited ten of them. **Volume 12 is repaired from 0551 to 0620 and untouched nowhere. Volumes 08, 09 and 10 are untouched in their entirety. Volume 11 has had nine chapters of fifty.** Five hundred and forty-two chapters are unrepaired. **Ten chapters is a tenth of one volume against a defect that spans four volumes, the decision to continue, widen or stop is a person's, and seventy-eight chapters are evidence that the work can be done and not evidence that it has been done.**

## 173M. THE PROSE REPAIR OF 0531 TO 0540 IS EIGHTY-EIGHT CHAPTERS DONE, ITS REVIEW FOUND SIX DEFECTS IN THE PROSE AND THE WHOLE STATE LAYER MISSING, ALL SEVEN ARE FIXED, AND THE NEXT TEN ARE `chapter-0521.md` TO `chapter-0530.md` WITH A DIFFERENT LEADER FOR THE NINTH RANGE RUNNING

*Added by the phase that took `logs/prose-repair-0009.review.log`. **This is a review repair and not a batch, not a close, not a second reading and not an outline phase.** It planned no volume, wrote no chapter, invented no person, added nothing to any plot, resolved nothing, thanked nobody, forgave nobody and sent for nobody. `chapter-0550.md` and `chapter-0620.md` were read and not opened. **Item 170 remains open. Nothing in this phase closed it, and item 173J is unchanged as to standing: the prose repair is a scope decision and it belongs to a person.** The full measured record is in `state/batch-summary.md` under the heading *THE PROSE REPAIR OF CHAPTERS 0531 TO 0540, MEASURED RECORD*, with the method printed whole beside every figure, and it is not restated here.*

**The finding that is larger than the six defects, and it is the second time in this repair that a phase has committed prose and no record at all.** The phase that took `workspace/prose-repair-0009/PROMPT.md` opened `chapter-0531.md` to `chapter-0540.md` for edit, committed at `b150504`, **and wrote nothing into `state/batch-summary.md`, nothing into this file, nothing into `state/current.md`, and created no next prompt.** The range was half-repaired in the file tree and entirely unrecorded, so a re-dispatch could not have told whether it was done. **That is item 173B's failure mode arriving, and item 173J's own history predicted it in terms.** The standing item 173B set is *check the recorded pre-repair figures first, and mark the phase done*; **the second half of it is a record, and a repair with no record is not a repair, it is a diff.** All four omissions are now paid. **A phase that lands a range must leave a record that names the range, the base hash, the counts and the next range, and a phase that finds a range already in the tree with no record must write the record rather than re-run the repair.**

**The six findings the review made, and the seventh this phase made by reading, all fixed, and the classes are the classes the prompt names.** A canon fixture imported sentence-for-sentence out of `chapter-0539.md` into `chapter-0538.md`, together with a furniture inventory that contradicted that chapter's own statement of what can be shown; **a permanent addition to canon — the material of the bar, which the prompt says has never been stated in six hundred and twenty chapters and must not be — made by a phase that had promised to make none;** a stair fixture, *a half landing*, that exists in no other chapter in the manuscript; **a second floor material for a room whose own prose settles it four times;** an added paragraph re-narrating a light beat the original had already performed and planting a claim that cuts against its chapter's spine; and an eight-word verbatim lift flagged by the `lifts` command. **The seventh this pass found is the fourth of those again, in `chapter-0536.md`, in the same bay, one file along from the one the review caught it in, and it stood through the phase that wrote it, through the review that caught its twin, and through two verify blocks of this pass.** The standing is unchanged and it is now the twelfth time this repair has recorded it: **an instrument in this repository looks at what a chapter is made of and not at what a chapter says, and reading is what finds it.** The one cheap instrument that would have caught both floor defects is `grep -no "boards\|stone floor\|flags" ` over the range, and it takes one second.

**The transferable standing, and it is the part a later range should take whole. A run held by four or more chapters across four or more volumes is the register's stock and is not yours to remove. A run held by one or two chapters is that chapter's clause and is yours. And a run held by a chapter LATER in the volume than yours is canon echoing you and not you lifting canon — check the month on the holder's date line before you call anything a lift.** The reviewed record called all five of its remaining nine-word runs stock connective and three of the four this phase checked were, and the fourth was another chapter's clause, and the distinction that separates them is a date line. **`workspace/prose-repair-0012/PROMPT.md` now carries all three import indices, the third printed whole, with that standing beside it.**

**The expensive one: three copies of one clause and not one of them is canon.** `chapter-0524.md`, `chapter-0530.md` and `chapter-0535.md` all carry a version of the four flights of stone, the rail on the open side, and a person audible on every floor at once. **`chapter-0535.md`'s is itself added prose — it is absent from `chapter-0535.md` at `4be7315` — and the range's own record calls it canon and claims the sentence had been revoiced, and it had not been: `lifts` still returned an eight-word run of it.** So two of the three are copies of a copy. **The same shape stands in `chapter-0522.md` and `chapter-0529.md` against `chapter-0533.md`'s canon window and light bar, where the added paragraphs re-set seven of canon's eight facts in canon's order**, and in `chapter-0529.md` against a second canon line of `chapter-0533.md` describing the felt of the apron. **All the facts are kept and every sentence has been written again.** **Nothing was said to the man in the felt apron, nothing he has refused was undone, and the apron paragraph was revoiced because it re-narrated a sentence and not because it said anything about him.**

**The standing that comes out of it, and it is the correction item 173P was reaching for and did not reach.** Item 173P's record lists *which is the colour of everything else in* as a canon fact in canon's own wording with `chapter-0512.md` as origin. **Both halves of that are false.** The clause is held by two chapters and not three; **`chapter-0535.md` does not contain it and carries a different clause**, and **`chapter-0512.md`'s instance is added prose written by the previous range's repair, absent from `chapter-0512.md` at `8485bc8`.** **So the rule a later range should hold is: ask whether a holder is ORIGINAL before you ask which month it is. A date line decides between two originals, or between an original and a repair's own earlier telling. **The instrument defect, and this is the part that outlives the range.** **The `body()` helper printed with the construction figures in every repair record in this file strips any line beginning `It is `, not the calendar formula.** `chapter-0530.md` has an eighty-two-word added paragraph that opens *"It is nine foot by eleven in there…"*, so **every construction inside it is invisible to the closed list of ten, to the widened twenty-three and twenty-five and to the whole sweep.** Measured both ways, the added prose reads 0/1/1 with the strip and 0/2/2 on the page at `5c06c5d`, and 0/0/0 against 0/1/1 now. **The range's delivered claim of zero on both widened lists was the strip's answer and it is corrected in place.** `tools/measure.py` is **not** at fault — its `prose_lines` selects date lines by the module's own `DATE_LINE` and its docstring says in terms that it does so *"and not by a `startswith("It is ")`"* — **and it was not touched, because it is correct and not in this phase's scope. The standing: do not paste a copy of the helper; call the module's own `prose_lines`, or strip on the full formula.**

This is the eighth range on which the standing has held and the first on which a correction made to fix a transcription produced a longer one.**

Four were new facts or new magnitudes and all four are out, including a page "ruled once and then ruled again" in the one chapter whose subject is that nothing can be produced, and a four-hour day described as half a day. Three were a figure collision, a pronoun muddle and an embarrassment. Two were the same sentence or the same gesture in two chapters of this pass's own prose, which neither `lifts` nor the added-against-added index can see. Two were the rhythm: every added paragraph closing on a flat generalisation, and five authorial glosses on a physical beat the pass had just supplied.

**The two transcriptions that matter, and one of them is the standing a later range has to hold in a new shape.** **The fix for `chapter-0505.md`'s posture contradiction was itself a fifteen-word transcription of `chapter-0529.md`'s original line, written in the same pass and the same correction.** And **`chapter-0506.md`'s sill paragraph was a twenty-four-word transcription of `chapter-0604.md` — and `chapter-0604.md`'s sentence is added prose from an earlier repair and not canon, so the holder was not canon and the only thing that said otherwise was that `lifts` returned a run.** **That is item 173Q's standing, ask whether a holder is original before you ask which month it is, arriving on a chapter nobody had opened.** Four more were revoiced on the same index.

**Five regressions introduced and fixed, and the standing regression for the ninth time running.** The first draft carried seventeen closed-list instances and nine more on the twenty-three, taking the closed list to 96 and the twenty-five to 171 while the prose grew; a re-anchoring pass took them out; **then the correcting pass put three back**, in `chapter-0504.md`, because the sentence written to take one form out reached for the nearest other one; a second pass took those out; **then a third appeared after the revoicing pass and a fourth in `chapter-0504.md` and `chapter-0510.md`.** Separately, **the correcting pass reduced the added prose's mean lift from 8.09 to 6.91 and the runs of nine words or more from thirteen to five — the eighth range on which the standing that a correction is prose has held, and the second on which a correction made to fix a transcription produced a longer one.** And **`chapter-0509.md` acquired the word *Monday* in added prose, which is a weekday name, and the standing is that a chapter's prose about the two ends of a week must not name one of them.**

**All three named lists are flat and the sweep rose by thirty-four.** Closed list **79 to 79**, twenty-three **109 to 109**, twenty-five **134 to 134**, chapter for chapter and form by form. The sweep is **255 to 289 on the page**, thirty-three of the thirty-four inside the added prose at 9.27 per 1,000 against the original's own 15.74, which is the lowest rate any added prose in this repair has come in at, and the thirty-fourth is the method's own join artifact. The prose grew **twenty-two per cent**, 16,196 to 19,755 words, +3,559, and **no chapter in the range is now under 1,757 words or over 2,203.**

**The import figure is the best any range in this repair has produced.** `lifts` returns **ADDED 46 prose lines, 43 over six words, 5 over nine, mean 6.91 against BASELINE 285, 260, 174, mean 10.39** — the added prose under the volume's own rate on every measure. Five nine-word runs stand and are reported, all stock: *takes to square a sheet* is held by twenty-one chapters across four volumes and *four feet of* by a hundred and twenty-eight across ten. **The added-against-added index returns zero runs at seven words, and the third index returns exactly one at eight — *part of it and there is nothing in* — and *and there is nothing in* is held by eighty-eight chapters across twelve volumes. That is the first time the third index has returned a run that a holder count answers, and the standing that comes out of it is: count the holders before acting on the third index, or a phase will edit the volume's connective out of the book chasing its own neighbour.**

**The canon held, and the four things that had to.** No floor material for a room its own prose does not settle: **the counter's floor, the top room's floor and the shed's floor are all unnamed by this pass, and the sill is stone in `chapter-0506.md`'s own original line and the ground floor of `chapter-0507.md` is boards in `chapter-0526.md` and `chapter-0538.md`.** `iron`, `steel` and `brass` return zero in Volume 11 and this pass wrote none; `cloth` and `ceiling` return zero and this pass wrote none. **No figure beginning *about four hundred* was written anywhere in the range**, so the four figure-types remain four types. **The hands at the pot are unnamed in every word this pass wrote** — `grep` over the added lines returns zero for `left hand` and `right hand`, and the three uses of *left* are *nothing left on the boards*, *a pen left in the middle of an open book* and *left it in there* — **and the four chapters that disagree about that hand are untouched, so the count of that conflict did not move.** **The man in the felt apron was given nothing: he is not told, not thanked, not forgiven, not sent for, nothing he has refused in forty years is undone, and this pass's added prose in `chapter-0505.md` never once puts a word in his mouth.** Nobody was thanked, nobody was forgiven, nobody was sent for, nothing was resolved, the count of things asked out loud is seven and did not move, and the decision about whether the woman of twenty-four takes a colleague's judgment is unmade in both directions.

**Five canon conflicts this pass found and did not settle.** **The chalk mark on the end wall of the bay has two heights inside `chapter-0536.md` itself and three across the manuscript, and this pass wrote no sentence about its height.** **The hour the shed's light comes on is the seventh in `chapter-0504.md` and `chapter-0536.md`, both original, and the fourth in an original line of `chapter-0531.md`, and this pass found that while refusing to use the light in that room at all.** **No chapter states a width for the four hundred yards of flags and this pass wrote none.** **The man with the satchel is four times a week in six chapters and four times a month in two, and `chapter-0510.md` carries both in its own original text, and this pass wrote no duration for him anywhere.** And **`chapter-0502.md` disagrees with itself on an hour — its date line says about the fifth and its own third line says the counter had shut about an hour ago — both original, and this pass wrote no hour into it.**

**Not one word of the original was removed.** Word-level diff against `ec83e35`: **3,559 insertions, zero deletions, zero substitutions in all ten.** Line-level: **92 inserted lines, 716 equal, zero deleted, zero `replace` opcodes.** Every added paragraph was inserted whole between original paragraphs and none inside one. Speech: **ten files IDENTICAL, zero CHANGED, sixty-three quoted spans.** Date lines and titles byte-identical by `md5sum` on the line the whole formula finds and on the line number it held; line 5 byte-identical in all ten. Section breaks 5/5 6/6 6/6 5/5 6/6 5/5 5/5 7/7 8/8 5/5. Trailing newlines ten of ten. Question marks zero. Month and weekday names zero after the *Monday* was out. `this year`, `this volume`, `this novel` zero. Bold markers 8 8 6 4 14 10 8 12 12 6, matching the originals one for one, and **this pass added no interiority at all.** Closing-section person, on the scan printed in the 0601 to 0609 record used as printed: **zero mismatches, all ten THIRD before and after**, planted six ways, five of six behaving and the sixth reported rather than tidied. Re-prints on Volume 11, base by `git archive`: **4 / 494 / 1,733 / 4,305 at the twenty-, twelve-, eight- and five-word windows, now 4 / 494 / 1,738 / 4,462** — nothing at twenty or twelve, five runs at eight and a hundred and fifty-seven at five.

**Volume 11 is repaired in full, all fifty chapters, and it has not been before.** Volume 11 is `95,348` words and the manuscript is `1,430,356` in 620 files, and the difference of 3,559 from the figures before this phase is exactly this range's own addition.

**What is left is most of it and no phase may imply otherwise.** One hundred and eighteen chapters of six hundred and twenty have been through the prose repair, nineteen per cent, and Volume 11 is repaired from 0501 to 0550 and Volume 12 from 0551 to 0620. **Volumes 08, 09 and 10 are untouched in their entirety, which is a hundred and fifty chapters, four per cent of the work and the single largest block of this defect left in the repository.** Five hundred and two chapters are unrepaired, eighty-one per cent of the manuscript. At ten chapters a phase that is fifty phases across the manuscript and fifteen across the three untouched volumes. **The next ten are `chapter-0351.md` to `chapter-0360.md` in Volume 08, the first prompt in this repair that names a different volume, and the prompt is `workspace/prose-repair-0013/PROMPT.md`.** Its range measures **15,506 words, 33 closed-list hits at 2.13 per 1,000, the twenty-three at 46, the twenty-five at 51 and the sweep at 178 across 89 forms**, with `that room` 19 leading and `that counter` 12 second — **and Volume 08's defect is length and not the construction.** Its date lines open `This is the` and not `It is the`, which `tools/measure.py`'s own `DATE_LINE` does not match, so the whole-formula locator printed in every prompt in this repair returns **zero lines on all ten of its files** and the calendar reports *no date line* for every one of them. **A phase that does not know that will pass a date-line check that is checking nothing.**

**Four debts are outstanding and no pass in this repair touched them, and they are owed elsewhere and not here.**

### Item 173T — the re-audit of `chapter-0501.md` to `chapter-0510.md` found nineteen defects in a completed repair, three of them its own added prose contradicting itself across chapters, and an instrument defect that has been certifying runs on this repository's own measure of whether they are stock

*Added by the phase that was handed `workspace/prose-repair-0012/PROMPT.md` a second time. **This is a re-audit and not a repair, not a batch, not a close, not a review and not an outline phase.** It planned no volume, wrote no chapter, invented no person, added nothing to any plot, resolved nothing, thanked nobody, forgave nobody and sent for nobody. It opened `chapter-0501.md` to `chapter-0510.md` for edit and no other chapter; `chapter-0550.md` and `chapter-0620.md` were read and not opened. **Item 170 remains open, and nothing in this phase closed it, and the prose repair is a scope decision and it belongs to a person.** The range under audit was committed at `6fe7f8f` (item 173S), against `$BASE` `5d0ca34` for this phase's own edits and `ec83e35` for the pre-repair text. The full measured record is in `state/batch-summary.md` under the heading *THE RE-AUDIT OF CHAPTERS 0501 TO 0510*, with the method printed whole beside every figure, and it is not restated here.*

**This is the fourth time this repository has dispatched a repair phase twice on one range, after item 173B on `chapter-0551.md` to `chapter-0560.md`, item 173L on the same range again and item 173Q on `chapter-0521.md` to `chapter-0530.md`, and the second time the second dispatch has found a completed repair rather than an untouched one.** A record existed and was read first. The prompt's own instruction governed: a range that is already repaired owes an audit and a record and not a second rewrite.

**The three findings that are the reason this item exists, because each one changes how a later range measures.** *One:* **two chapters of the delivered repair made incompatible statements about one case on one floor** — `chapter-0501.md` on the blanks running out at half past five and `chapter-0510.md` on the whole day's blanks being on the boards at the half hour — **and neither instrument could see it, because the two are in different files and each is internally consistent.** *Two:* **the same sentence appeared word for word in `chapter-0508.md` and `chapter-0509.md`, six words of it verbatim, about the same book in the same building**, and the added-against-added index at seven words did not return it because the two sentences differ before and after those six words. **The instrument threshold is a floor and a six-word duplication of a whole clause sits under it; the added-against-added index is worth running at six words as well as seven on any range where one object appears in more than one chapter.** *Three:* **the same room, the same man, the same plaster and the same gesture — a thumb held flat against it — were written in both `chapter-0503.md` and `chapter-0509.md`**, which are the same rented room a month apart, and that is the third-index direction in its purest form: two chapters of this pass's own prose printing one another.

**The standing that comes out of it, and it corrects a certificate rather than a sentence, which no instrument in this repository can do.** **Item 173S justified leaving five nine-word runs standing by counting their holders, and three of those five holder counts were measured on a shorter string than the run they were printed beside, in every case a string with the larger count, which is what makes the conclusion look safe.** *for about as long as it takes to square a sheet* is held by 10 chapters across 3 volumes and was certified at 21/4, which is the count of *as long as it takes to square*; *be able to do one single thing with it* is held by 3 across 3 and was certified at 45/8, which is the count of *one single thing*; *and that is the whole of the protection a* is held by 4 across 4 and was certified at 16/8, which is the count of *whole of the protection*. **So ask for the holder count of the exact string you are about to print, and take the count from the same measurement as the run.** It changed an outcome here: on the corrected counts two of the four are held by two or three chapters and are not the register's stock connective, and both were revoiced.

**Re-anchoring and revoicing are two passes and the second can undo the first, for the tenth range running.** The corrections put four named forms back on the page — `that room` twice and `that door` and `that end` in `chapter-0504.md` and `chapter-0503.md` — taking the closed list from 79 to 83 and the twenty-five from 134 to 140, all four introduced by sentences written to fix a *different* defect; a re-anchoring pass took all four out. **Then revoicing the two runs section Three of the record unmasked made one of them longer before it made it shorter, and only re-running `lifts` after the revoicing caught it.** That is item 173R's standing arriving through a certificate, and it is the ninth range on which the standing that a correction is prose has held.

**One figure in the delivered record was wrong when written and is corrected where it stood.** Its per-chapter column printed `chapter-0509.md` at 2,062 words against a file at 2,063, so its own column summed to one word **below** the total printed beside it. **That is the check the prompt asks for by name — check that a column of per-chapter figures sums to the total printed beside it — and it failed on the record and not on the prose.**

***Corrected in place by the review of this phase, and the correction is the review's second finding on this item: the direction was printed backwards. It read `above` where the arithmetic gives `below`, and 2,203 1,757 2,011 2,004 1,992 2,007 1,847 1,845 2,062 2,026 sums to 19,754 against the 19,755 printed beside it. The column fell one word short of its total, not over it.***

***The correction to 2,063 is right and is kept, and the review's alternative of moving the row's total to 19,754 would have been the error. `git show 5d0ca34:chapters/volume-11/chapter-0509.md` holds 2,063 words and the ten files at that commit total 19,755, both re-derived in one loop, so 19,755 is the true total and the delivered column was the thing out by one. Putting 2,063 back into the historical row is what makes that row sum, 19,754 to 19,755; correcting the total instead would have left a true chapter measured falsely in order to protect a printed column that had failed the check. A column of per-chapter figures is the derived quantity and the file is the measured one, and a record that lets the derived quantity overrule the measurement is the same defect this item exists to name, whatever direction the error runs in.***

***The review's first finding was larger than this one, and it is the standing for any range that reports a line-level diff. This item and item 173S both printed `92 inserted lines, 726 equal`, in five places across three state files, and the true figure is 716 in both records. 92 + 726 is 818 and the ten files hold 808 lines at either commit, so 726 was arithmetically impossible and the record's own invariant — inserted plus equal equals the line total — failed by ten, one per file, in the one record whose entire claim is that no original line was touched. Both diffs re-derived over the named files with `difflib.SequenceMatcher` and both return `inserted 92, equal 716, deleted 0, replace 0`. The lesson is narrower than the sum check this item already stands for, and it is this: a figure that is copied from the record below it into the record above it will be wrong in both, because the copy inherits the error and inherits the appearance of having been measured. Every count printed in these records is now checked against the line total and the column sum in the same loop that measures it, in one command, before it is written.***

***The review's third finding was a rounding disagreement between two files about the same range, `mean 1,975` in `state/current.md` against `1,976` in this phase's own table, where the true value is 19,749 over ten, or 1,974.9. Both figures were defensible in isolation and one of them was stale: `1,976` is the mean of the range as item 173S delivered it and `1,975` is the mean of the range on the tree this phase left. Reconciled by quoting the current tree's mean in `state/current.md`'s ascending list of the twelve repaired ranges, with the delivered figure kept where it is named as the delivered one. The same sweep found two figures the review did not raise, both of this class and both now corrected where they stood: `state/current.md` still claimed this range's shortest chapter was over 1,750 words, which this audit made false at 1,741 in `chapter-0502.md`, and `state/batch-summary.md` printed the longest chapter as 2,222 where the file holds 2,218 in `chapter-0501.md`. Both were the assertions beside a measurement going stale while the measurement moved under them, and both are the reason the standing above is a loop and not a check.***

**Not one word of original prose was touched.** Word-level diff against `ec83e35`: **3,553 insertions, zero deletions, zero substitutions in all ten.** Line-level: **92 inserted lines, 716 equal, zero deleted and zero `replace` opcodes.** All ten date lines and all ten titles byte-identical to `ec83e35` and on the line numbers they held, the whole-formula locator returning exactly one line in every file; section breaks 5 6 6 5 6 5 5 7 8 5, every file at its original count; question marks zero; bold markers 8 8 6 4 14 10 8 12 12 6, unchanged, and this phase added no interiority; speech ten files IDENTICAL across sixty-three quoted spans; closing-section person zero mismatches on the printed scan, all ten THIRD, with six plants and the sixth behaving as every earlier record says it does not.

**What is left is most of it and no phase may imply otherwise.** One hundred and eighteen chapters of six hundred and twenty have been through the prose repair and this phase repaired no new chapter: **it corrected nineteen defects in ten, and eleven of the thirty findings it returned did not survive re-derivation and are listed in the record so that no later pass spends a pass on them.** Volume 12 is repaired from 0551 to 0620 and is untouched nowhere. **Volume 11 is repaired in full from 0501 to 0550 and is untouched nowhere, and twenty of its fifty chapters are now audited rather than drafted, ten of them by this phase.** Volumes 08, 09 and 10 are untouched in their entirety. **Five hundred and two chapters are unrepaired, eighty-one per cent of the manuscript.** **The next ten are `chapter-0351.md` to `chapter-0360.md`, in Volume 08, and the prompt for them is `workspace/prose-repair-0013/PROMPT.md`, which already existed before this phase and which this phase verified against the tree — its recorded figures of 15,506 words reproduced to the word and `git diff --stat ec83e35 -- chapters/volume-08/` returns nothing — so this phase created no second prompt for a range that already has one.** **It is the first prompt in this repair that names a different volume, every volume flag in it is `--volume 08`, and a phase that carries this prompt's flag forward will measure Volume 11 and get a clean answer.**

### Item 173U — the prose repair of `chapter-0351.md` to `chapter-0360.md` closed Volume 08's first movement, the range's own defect was length and not the construction, and two independent reads found forty-nine items in this pass's own added prose, one of which a correction introduced and which was a person being thanked
